O Mitra and Varuṇa, approaching you two, the well known and ancient, for protection, I praise both of you together. I extol you, who have excellent horses and excellent understanding, with exceeding praise for the sake of food.

##### English translation
Approaching you, ancient (divinities), I invoke you together for protection : possessed of good steeds, (we praise you) who are provident to give us food.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मि॒त्रो अं॒होश्चि॒दादु॒रु क्षया॑य गा॒तुं व॑नते ।  
> मि॒त्रस्य॒ हि प्र॒तूर्व॑तः सुम॒तिरस्ति॑ विध॒तः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 39

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मित्रः । अंहोः । चित् । आत् । उरु । क्षयाय । गातुं । वनते ।  
> मित्रस्य । हि । प्रतूर्वतः । सुमतिः । अस्ति । विधतः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मित्रो देवोऽहोश्चित् अंहस्वतोऽपि स्तोतुरादनंतरमेवोरूरवे
> क्षयाय गृहाय निवासाय वा गातुमुपायं वनते । प्रयच्छति । मित्रस्य हि मित्रस्य
> खलु देवस्य सुमतिः शोभनबुद्धिः प्रतूर्वतः प्रकर्षण हिंसतोऽपि विधतः परिचरतः
> शुशूषकस्यास्ति हि । भवति खलु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The god Mitra, even for a man who is a sinner (अंहोश्चित् (*aṃhościt*)), who is a praiser, immediately afterwards (आत् (*āt*)) grants (वनते (*vanate*), gives) the means (गातुं (*gātuṃ*), a way) for a spacious dwelling, a house or a place to live. For the good will (सुमतिः (*sumatiḥ*)) of the god Mitra is certainly (हि (*hi*)) with the servant, the attendant who serves, even one who greatly (प्रतूर्वतः (*pratūrvataḥ*)) injures. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अंहोश्चित् (*aṃhościt*)—even if he is a sinful person
- आत् (*āt*)—as soon as he recites the praise
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra
- उरु (*uru*)—spacious
- क्षयाय (*kṣayāya*)—for the house
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—the means of building (the way of entrance)
- वनते (*vanate*)—provides
- प्रतूर्वतः (*pratūrvataḥ*)—who destroys foes
- मित्रस्य (*mitrasya*)—of Mitra
- सुमतिः (*sumatiḥ*)—the gracious mind
- विधतः (*vidhataḥ*)—upon the one who offers worship
- अस्ति हि (*asti hi*)—is always present

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Even if he is a sinful person, as soon as he recites the praise Mitra provides for him a way of entrance into a spacious house. The gracious mind of Mitra, who destroys foes, is always present upon the one who offers worship.

##### English translation
Mitra grants even to the sinful (worshipper) the (means of) repairing to his spacious dwelling: the favour of Mitra, the destroyer of foes, is (granted) to (his) adorer.
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒यं मि॒त्रस्याव॑सि॒ स्याम॑ स॒प्रथ॑स्तमेः ।  
> अ॒ने॒हस॒स्त्वोत॑यः स॒त्रा वरु॑णशेषसः ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 40

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयं । मित्रस्य । अवसि । स्याम । सप्रथःतमे ।  
> अनेहसः । त्वाऊतयः । सत्रा । वरुणशेषसः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयं यजमाना मित्रस्य दुःखनिवारकस्य देवस्य सप्रथस्तमे सर्वतः
> पृथुतमेवसि रक्षणेवयं स्याम । भवेम । किंचानेहसोऽपापास्वोऽतयस्त्वया
> रक्षिता वरुणशेषसः । शेष इत्यपत्यनाम । वारकाः पुत्रा येषां ते । तादृशाः सत्रा
> वयं सर्वॆ सहैव भवेम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

We, the sacrificers, may we be in the protection of the god Mitra, remover of distress, that is the most widely extended (सप्रथस्तमे (*saprathastame*), broadest on every side). Further, free from sin (अनेहसः (*anehasaḥ*)) and protected by you (त्वोतयः (*tvotayaḥ*)), वरुणशेषसः (*varuṇaśeṣasaḥ*): शेष (*śeṣa*) is a name for offspring; they whose sons are protective. Being such, all of us together (सत्रा (*satrā*)) may we be.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्र (*mitra*)—(O Mitra)
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- अनेहसः (*anehasaḥ*)—free from sin
- त्वोतयः (*tvotayaḥ*)—under your protection
- वरुणशेषसः (*varuṇaśeṣasaḥ*)—good subjects belonging to Varuṇa (or having protecting sons)
- मित्रस्य (*mitrasya*)—of Mitra
- सप्रथस्तमे (*saprathastame*)—most abundant
- अवसि (*avasi*)—in the protection
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra, free from sin, under your protection and good subjects belonging to Varuṇa, may we obtain the most abundant protection of Mitra.

##### English translation
May we ever be in the comprehensive guardianship of Mitra, and free from sin, enjoy, (Mitra), your protection, being at the same time the children of Varuna.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वं मि॑त्रे॒मं जनं॒ यत॑थः॒ सं च॑ नयथः ।  
> मा म॒घोनः॒ परि॑ ख्यतं॒ मो अ॒स्माक॒मृषी॑णां गोपी॒थे न॑  
> उरुष्यतं ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 41

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवं । मित्रा । इमं । जनं । यतथः । सं । चॆ । नयथः ।  
> मा । मघोनः । परि । ख्यतं । मो इति । अस्माकं । ऋषीणां । गोऽपीथे । नः ।  
> उरुष्यतं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रा मित्रावरुणौ युवं युवामिमं जनं स्तोतारं मां प्रति
> यतथः । गच्छथः । आगत्य च सं च नयथः । संनयथश्च । कामान् प्रापयथः ।
> यद्वा । यतथ इत्यंतर्णीतण्यर्थोऽयं । अहनि यतथः । व्यापारेषु गमयथः ।
> रात्र्य च सं नयथः । निर्व्यापारं कारयथः । मघोनो हनिर्लक्षणान्नवतोऽस्मान्मा
> परि ख्यतं । मा परित्यजतं । मो मैव ऋषीणामस्माकं पुत्रानपि परि ख्यतं । किंतु
> गोपीथे । गौः सोमरसः । तस्य पानं यस्मिन् स गोपीथो यज्ञः । तस्मिन्न
> उरुष्यतं । रक्षतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra, that is, Mitra and Varuṇa, you two go (यतथः (*yatathaḥ*)) towards this person (जनं (*janaṃ*)), me the praiser; having come, you lead (नयथः (*nayathaḥ*)) him, that is, you cause desires to be reached. Or else: यतथः (*yatathaḥ*) has a causative sense within it. By day you make him go (यतथः (*yatathaḥ*)) into his undertakings, and by night you bring him to rest (सं च नयथः (*saṃ ca nayathaḥ*)), you make him idle. Do not abandon (मा परि ख्यतं (*mā pari khyataṃ*)) us, rich (मघोनः (*maghonaḥ*)) in oblations of food; do not abandon the sons also of us who are seers (ऋषीणां (*ṛṣīṇāṃ*)). But in the गोपीथे (*gopīthe*) (गौः (*gauḥ*) is the Soma juice; the sacrifice in which it is drunk is the गोपीथो (*gopītho*)), protect us (उरुष्यतं (*uruṣyataṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रा (*mitrā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- इमं जनं (*imaṃ janaṃ*)—looking to this praiser
- यतथः (*yatathaḥ*)—come
- सं च नयथः (*saṃ ca nayathaḥ*)—and provide what he desires
- मघोनः (*maghonaḥ*)—us who possess food in the form of oblations
- मा परि ख्यतं (*mā pari khyataṃ*)—do not abandon
- ऋषीणां (*ṛṣīṇāṃ*)—who are seers
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—to us
- मो (*mo*)—(our sons and others) do not reject
- गोपीथे (*gopīthe*)—in this sacrifice in which Soma is drunk
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- उरुष्यतं (*uruṣyataṃ*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, come towards this praiser. Provide what he desires. Do not abandon us, who possess food in the form of oblations. Do not reject our sons and others, we who are seers. Protect us in this sacrifice in which Soma is drunk.

##### English translation
You come Mitra and Varuna, to this man, and guide him (to his desires): deny us not when we are rich (in offerings) : (deny us not), who are (the sons) of Rishis : protect us in the presenting of the libation.

### Printed p. 42
## Sūkta 66 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning आ चिकितान (*ā cikitāna*) has six Ṛks and is the tenth sūkta. Its seer is Rātahavya; its deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; its metre is Anuṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇikā here says आ चिकितानानुष्टुभं (*ā cikitānānuṣṭubhaṃ*). The first tṛca (the first three Ṛks) is used in the Daśarātra, on the fourth day, in the प्रउगशस्त्रे (*praugaśastre*) of the Maitrāvaruṇa priest. It is also laid down in the sūtra आ चिकितान सुकृतू त्यमुः वो अप्रहणं (*ā cikitāna sukṛtū tyamuḥ vo aprahaṇaṃ*) (Ā. 7-11).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning "Ā cikitāna", is the tenth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has six Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Rātahavya of the Atri line; the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; the metre is Anuṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Ā cikitānānuṣṭubhaṃ tu". The first tṛca of this sūkta, that is, the first three Ṛks, is used in the ritual for the prauga-śastra mantras recited on the fourth day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra by the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa, as set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra in the sūtra "Ā cikitānā sukṛtū tyamu vo apraha ṇaṃ" (Ā. 7-11).

##### Particulars

- Sūkta: 66
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 66
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 4
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 6
- Ṛṣi: Rātahavya Ātreya
- Devatā: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Chandas: Anuṣṭubh
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ चि॑कितान सु॒क्रतू॑ दे॒वौ म॑र्त रि॒शाद॑सा ।  
> वरु॑णाय ऋ॒तपे॑शसे दधी॒त प्रय॑से म॒हे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । चिकितान । सुक्रतू इति सुक्रतू । देवौ । मर्त । रिशादसा ।  
> वरुणाय । ऋत्पेशसे । दधीत । प्रयसे । महे ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 43

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे चिकितान जानन् स्तुतिं हे मर्त मनुष्य रातहव्य त्वमिति तस्यैव
> संबोधनं । सुक्रतू शोभनकर्माणौ रिशादसा हिंसकानां हिंसकौ देवावा ।
> आकारयेति शेषः । आहूय च ऋतपेशसे । पेश इति रूपनाम । ऋतमुदकं ।
> उदकमेव रूपं यस्य तादृशाय वरुणाय प्रयसे ॥ मत्त्वर्थो लुप्यते ॥
> हविर्लक्षणान्नवते महे महते पूज्याय दधीत । दद्याः । हविरिति शेषः ।
> द्वितीयार्थे वा चतुर्थी । महद्धनिर्दधीत । महतोऽन्नस्य लाभायेति ना
> योज्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O चिकितान (*cikitāna*), you who know the praise; O मर्त (*marta*), man, Rātahavya: the word "you" is his own address. Call (आकारय (*ākāraya*), to be supplied) the two gods, सुक्रतू (*sukratū*), doers of good works, रिशादसा (*riśādasā*), destroyers of those who destroy. Having called them, give (दधीत (*dadhīta*), you should give; "oblation" is to be supplied) to Varuṇa ऋतपेशसे (*ṛtapeśase*): पेश (*peśa*) is a name for form, ऋत (*ṛta*) is water, so one whose form is water. प्रयसे (*prayase*): the affix expressing possession is dropped; so, possessed of food in the form of oblation. महे (*mahe*): great, worshipful. Or else the dative stands in the sense of the accusative. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- चिकितान (*cikitāna*)—you who know the order of worship
- मर्त (*marta*)—O man (praiser)
- सुक्रतू (*sukratū*)—the performers of excellent works
- रिशादसा (*riśādasā*)—and destroyers of foes
- देवौ (*devau*)—Mitra and Varuṇa
- आ (*ā*)—invite
- ऋतपेशसे (*ṛtapeśase*)—who has water itself as his form
- प्रयसे (*prayase*)—who is a sharer in food
- महे (*mahe*)—and is worshipful
- वरुणाय (*varuṇāya*)—to Varuṇa
- दधीत (*dadhīta*)—offer the oblation

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O man, praiser, who have knowledge of the order of worship, invite Mitra and Varuṇa, the performers of excellent works and destroyers of foes. Offer the oblation to Varuṇa, who has water itself as his form, who is a sharer in food in the form of oblations, and who is worshipful.

##### English translation
Man, endowed with intelligence, (adore) the two deities, the performers of good deeds, the destroyers of foes; offer (oblations) to the adorable accepter of (sacrificial) food, to Varuna, whose form is water.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता हि क्ष॒त्रमवि॑ह्रुतं स॒म्यग॑सु॒र्य१॒॑ १ माशा॑ते ।  
> अध॑ व्र॒तेव॒ मानु॑षं॒ स्व१॒॑र्ण धा॑यि दर्श॒तं ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 44

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । हि । क्षत्रं । अविऽह्रुतं । सम्यक् । आसुर्यं । आशाते इति ।  
> अध । व्रताइव । मानुषं । स्वः । न । धायि । दर्शतं ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता हि । हीति हेत् । यस्मात्ता तौ क्षत्रं बलमविह्रुतमहिंस्यमसुर्यमसुरविघाति महद्बलं सम्यगाशाते व्याप्नुतः अध अतो हेतोर्मानुषं
> मनुष्येषु प्रवृत्तं व्रतेव कर्मेव तद्मथा मनुष्येषु नियतं स्वर्ण सूर्य इव स
> यथा दिवि दृश्यते तद्वद्दर्शतं दर्शनीयं बलं धायि । यज्ञे निहितं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ता हि (*tā hi*): हि (*hi*) gives the reason. Since those two have strength (क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)), unharmed (अविह्रुतं (*avihrutaṃ*)) and आसुर्यं (*āsuryaṃ*), which strikes down the asuras, great strength, and pervade it fully (सम्यगाशाते (*samyagāśāte*)), therefore (अध (*adha*)): the strength that is directed to men, fixed among men like a rite (व्रतेव (*vrateva*)), and like the sun (स्वर्ण (*svarṇa*)) as he is seen in heaven, so beautiful (दर्शतं (*darśataṃ*)) strength is placed (धायि (*dhāyi*)) in the sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ता (*tā*)—those Mitra and Varuṇa
- हि (*hi*)—because
- अविह्रुतं (*avihrutaṃ*)—unresisted
- असुरं (*asuraṃ*)—and destroying the asuras
- क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—strength
- सम्यक् (*samyak*)—fully
- आशाते (*āśāte*)—they have obtained
- अध (*adha*)—for that very reason
- मानुषं (*mānuṣaṃ*)—fixed among men
- व्रता इव (*vratā iva*)—like rites
- स्वः न (*svaḥ na*)—and like the sun established in the sky
- दर्शतं (*darśataṃ*)—attractive
- धायि (*dhāyi*)—power has been established (in the sacrifice)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Because Mitra and Varuṇa have fully obtained strength that is unresisted and destroys the asuras, for that very reason attractive power has been established in the sacrifice, like the rites fixed among men and like the sun established in the sky.

##### English translation
Inasmuch as you two are possessed of irresistible and Asura-subduing strength, therefore has holy sacrifice been established amongst men, as the sun (has been placed) in the sky.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता वा॒मेषे॒ रथा॑नामु॒र्वीं गव्यू॑तिमेषां ।  
> रा॒तह॑व्यस्य सुष्टु॒तिं द॒धृक्सोमै॑र्मनामहे ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 45

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । वां । एषे । रथानां । उर्वीं । गव्यूतिं । एषां ।  
> रात्‌हव्यस्य । सुस्तुतिं । दधृक् । स्तोमैः । मनामहे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणौ ता तौ प्रसिद्धौ नां युवां रथानामस्मदीयानामेषां ।
> पुरतो वर्तमानानामुर्विं प्रभूतां गव्यूतिमतिविस्तृतं मार्गमेषे गंतुं ।
> मार्गरक्षणायेत्यर्थः ॥ तुमर्थेऽसेन्प्रत्ययः ॥ तदर्थं नां मनामहे स्तुमो
> रातहव्यस्य ऋषे रातहव्यसंबंधिभिः सुष्टुतिं सुष्टुतिभिः शोभनस्तुतिसाधनैः
> स्तोमैः दधृक् धर्षकौ युवां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses Mitra and Varuṇa, "those well known ones, you". For the sake of our chariots that stand in front, for going along a broad, abundant and very extensive path (the meaning is: for the protection of the way), we praise you. We praise you with the praises of Rātahavya, that is, with the good praises, the means of good eulogy, connected with Rātahavya the seer, you who are bold.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एषां रथानां (*eṣāṃ rathānāṃ*)—to these chariots of ours, in front
- उर्विं (*urviṃ*)—wide
- गव्यूतिं (*gavyūtiṃ*)—path
- ऐषे (*aiṣe*)—in order to travel
- रातहव्यस्य (*rātahavyasya*)—of the seer called Rātahavya
- सुष्टुतिं (*suṣṭutiṃ*)—the excellent worship
- स्तोमैः (*stomaiḥ*)—with his praises
- दधृक् (*dadhṛk*)—those who accept
- ता (*tā*)—who are well known
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- मनामहे (*manāmahe*)—we praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, in order to travel while protecting the broad path in front of these chariots of ours, we praise you, who are well known and who accept the excellent worship of the seer called Rātahavya, together with his praises.

##### English translation
We glorify you both, that your chariots may precede ours by a long distance; accepting the pious worship of Rathahavya with (his) praises.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अधा॒ हि काव्या॑ यु॒वं दक्ष॑स्य पू॒र्भिर॑द्भुता ।  
> नि के॒तुना॒ जना॑नां चि॒के,थे॑ पूतदक्षसा ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 46

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध । हि । काव्या । युवं । दक्षस्य । पूःभिः । अद्भुता ।  
> नि । केतुना । जनानां । चिकेथे इति । पूतदक्षसा ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अध अपि च । हि प्रसिद्धौ । काव्या स्तुत्यौ युवं युवां दक्षस्य प्रवृद्धस्य मम
> पूर्भिः पूरकैः स्तवैर्हे अद्भुतामहांतावाश्चर्यभूत् संतौ केतुना प्रज्ञानेनानुकूलेन मनसा जनानां । यजमानानामित्यर्थः । स्तोत्रं नि चिकेथे । नितरां
> जानीथः । हे पूतदक्षसा शुद्धबलौ युवां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Moreover": you two, well known and praiseworthy, with the fulfilling praises of me, who am grown strong, O wonderful ones, that is, great and marvellous beings, know thoroughly, with a favourable understanding and mind, the praise of the people, that is, of the sacrificers. The meaning is: you know it fully. O you of pure strength.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध (*adha*)—moreover
- काव्या (*kāvyā*)—worthy of praise
- पूतदक्षसा (*pūtadakṣasā*)—possessed of purified strength
- दक्षस्य (*dakṣasya*)—of me, who am strong (and worship you)
- पूर्भिः (*pūrbhiḥ*)—with gratifying praises
- अद्भुता (*adbhutā*)—astonished ones
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—of these sacrificers, also the praise
- केतुना (*ketunā*)—with a gracious understanding
- नि चिकेथे (*ni cikethe*)—know fully (and be gratified)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, worthy of praise and possessed of purified strength, astonished by the gratifying praises of me, who am strong and worship you, know fully, with a gracious understanding, the praise of these sacrificers also, and be gratified.

##### English translation
Now, adorable and wonderful deities, (propitiated) by the former (praises) of (me, your) worshipper, do you, who are of pure vigour, consider with approving minds (the adoration) of these men
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तद्व॒तं पृ॑थिवि बृ॒हच्छ्र॑वए॒ष ऋषी॑णां ।  
> ज्र॒य॒सा॒नावरं॑ पृ॒थ्विति॑ क्षरंति॒ याम॑भिः ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 47

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । ऋतं । पृथिवि । बृहत् श्रवः एषे । ऋषीणां ।  
> ज्रयसान् । अरं । पृथु । अति । क्षरंति । यामभिः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पृथिवि देवि त्वयि तत्सर्वैरर्थ्यमानत्वेन प्रसिद्धं बृहत् प्रभूतमृतमुदकमृषीणां स्तोत्यणामस्माकं श्रवएषेऽन्नस्यैषणे सति जयसान् गच्छंतौ ॥
> छंदस्यसानच् शुज्य भ्यां । उ. २-८६ । इत्यसानच् ज्रयतेरपि भवति । चित इत्यंतोदात्तः । अरमलमत्यर्थं पृथु यथा भवति तथा यामभिर्गमनैरति क्षरंति । अत्यर्थं
> वर्षतः ॥ व्यत्ययेन बहुवचनं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O goddess Earth, in you" there is that well-known great water, sought by all, belonging to the seers, that is, to us the praisers. When the search for food is on, those two who go swiftly pour down rain exceedingly, sufficiently and abundantly, by their movements. The plural number is by the substitution of number (vyatyaya).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पृथिवि (*pṛthivi*)—O Earth
- ऋषीणां (*ṛṣīṇāṃ*)—of the seers
- श्रवएषे (*śravaeṣe*)—that which is chief for obtaining food
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant
- तत् (*tat*)—well known
- यतं (*yataṃ*)—the water**NOTE**
- ज्रयसान् (*jrayasān*)—Mitra and Varuṇa, who move swiftly
- यामभिः (*yāmabhiḥ*)—by their movements
- आरं (*āraṃ*)—so as to be a means of satisfaction
- पृथु (*pṛthu*)—abundantly
- अति क्षरंति (*ati kṣaraṃti*)—pour down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Earth, Mitra and Varuṇa, who move swiftly, pour down abundantly, by their movements, the well-known and abundant water, chief for obtaining food for the seers, so that it becomes a means of satisfaction for all.

##### English translation
Earth. in you is abundant water for the necessities of the Rishis : the two active (deities) dispense by thcir movements sufficiently copious (rain).
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ यद्वा॑मीयचक्षसा॒ मित्र॑ व॒यं च॑ सू॒रयः॑ ।  
> व्यचि॑ष्ठे बहु॒पाय्ये॒ यते॑महि स्व॒राज्ये॑ ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 48

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । यत् । मां । ईयचक्षसा । मित्रा । वयं । च । सूरयः ।  
> व्यचिष्ठे । बहुपाय्ये । यतेमहि । स्वराज्ये ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ईयचक्षसा व्याप्तदर्शनौ हे मित्रा मित्रावरुण् नां युवां यत् ये
> वयं च सूरयः स्तोतार आ आह्वयामः । व्यचिष्ठेत्यंतविस्त्यते बहुपाय्कॆ
> बहुभिर्गंतव्ये बहुभी रक्षितव्ये वा स्वराज्ये स्वराट् त्वाय एवंविधराज्यार्थं
> यतेमहि । गच्छेम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, of far-reaching sight, we who are the sūris, the praisers, call upon you. For the sake of such a kingdom of our own rule, that is, a kingdom to be entered by many or protected by many, we strive, that is, we go. Sāyaṇa glosses "vyaciṣṭhe" as very extensive.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ईयचक्षसा (*īyacakṣasā*)—possessed of a power of sight that pervades the whole universe
- मित्रा (*mitrā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- सूरयः (*sūrayaḥ*)—praisers
- यत् वयं (*yat vayaṃ*)—we, who are your devotees
- आ (*ā*)—call upon you
- व्यचिष्ठे (*vyaciṣṭhe*)—very extensive
- बहुपाय्कॆ (*bahupāykè*)—entered by many
- स्वराज्ये (*svarājye*)—for entering your kingdom
- यतेमहि (*yatemahi*)—we strive

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, whose power of sight pervades the whole universe, we, the praisers and your devotees, call upon you. We strive to enter your kingdom, which is very extensive and entered by many.

##### English translation
We and the devout (iuvoke) you, Mitra and Varuna, who are farseeing: may we proceed to your spacious and much frequented kingdom.
### Printed p. 49
## Sūkta 67 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> बळित्था देवेति पंचर्चमेकादशं सूक्तं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका । बळित्था
> पंच यजत इति । यजतो नामात्रेय ऋषिः । मित्रावरुणौ देवता । आनुष्टुभं
> त्वित्युक्तत्वादिदमप्यानुष्टुभं । विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is the eleventh sūkta, "baḷitthā deva", consisting of five Ṛks. The Anukramaṇikā says: "baḷitthā pañca yajataḥ". The seer is Yajata, of the Atri family; the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa. Since "Ānuṣṭubhaṃ tu" has been said, this sūkta also is in the Anuṣṭubh metre. The application is by indication (laiṅgika).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning "baḷitthā deva" is the eleventh sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has five Ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "baḷitthā pañca jagataḥ".**NOTE** For this sūkta the seer is the one called Yajata Ātreya; the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; the metre is Anuṣṭup. The application is by indication.

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 67
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 67
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 5
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5
- Seer: Yajata Ātreya
- Deity: Mitrāvaruṇā
- Metre: Anuṣṭup
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बळि॒त्था दे॑व निष्कृ॒तमादि॑त्या यज॒तं बृ॒हत् ।  
> वरु॑ण॒ मित्रार्य॑म॒न्वर्षि॑ष्टं क्ष॒त्रमा॑शाथे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बट् । इत्था । देवा । निःकृतं । आदित्या । यजतं । बृहत् ।  
> वरुण । मित्र । आर्यमन् । वर्षिष्ठं । क्षत्रं । आशाथे इति ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देवा द्योतमान् हे आदित्यादितेः पुत्रौ हे वरुण हे अर्यमन्
> अरीणां नियमितर्मित्र देव युवां बट् सत्यं निष्कृतमबाध्यमित्थेत्थमिदानीं-
> XX. 4

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O shining ones, O sons of Aditi, O Varuṇa, O Aryaman, the restrainer of enemies, O divine Mitra: you, in truth, in the way that obtains at present, have attained a strength that is flawless, that is, not to be obstructed.
### Printed p. 50

> वर्तमानप्रकारेण यजतं यष्टव्यं बृहदतिप्रवृद्धं वर्षिष्ठं प्रवृद्धतमं क्षत्रं
> बलमाशाथे । आश्नुवाथे । यद्वार्यमा पृथगेव निर्देष्टव्यः । द्विवचनं
> बहुवचनीकर्तव्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The strength you have attained is adorable, that is, worthy of worship, great, that is, exceedingly grown, and most vast; āśāthe means "you obtain". Or else Aryaman is to be indicated separately. The dual number is to be made plural.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवा (*devā*)—shining ones
- आदित्या (*ādityā*)—sons of Aditi
- वरुण अर‍्यमन् मित्र (*varuṇa aryaman mitra*)—O deities named Varuṇa, Aryaman and Mitra
- इत्था (*itthā*)—at this time of sacrifice
- बट् (*baṭ*)—truly
- निष्कृतं (*niṣkṛtaṃ*)—without deficiency
- यजतं (*yajataṃ*)—worthy of worship
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant
- वर्षिष्ठं (*varṣiṣṭhaṃ*)—exceedingly grown
- क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—strength
- आशाथे (*āśāthe*)—you have attained

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O shining ones, sons of Aditi, O deities named Varuṇa, Aryaman and Mitra, at this time of sacrifice you have attained a strength that is true, without deficiency, worthy of worship, abundant and exceedingly grown.

##### English translation
Divine sons of Aditi, Mitra, Varuna, Aryaman, verily you are posessed at the present time, of perfect, adorable, vast, exceeding strength.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ यद्योनिं॑ हिर॒ण्ययं॒ वरु॑ण॒ मित्र॒ सद॑थः ।  
> ध॒र्तारा॑ चर्षणी॒नां यं॒तं सु॒म्मं रि॑शादसा ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । यत् । योनिं । हिरण्ययं । वरुण । मित्र । सदथः ।  
> धर्तारा । चर्षणीनां । यंतं । सुम्नं । रिशादसा ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 51

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यस्माद्धिरण्ययं हितरमणीयं योनिं यज्ञभूमिं हे वरुण हे मित्र
> आ सदथः आगच्छथः तस्माच्चर्षणीनां मनुष्याणां धर्तारा धारकौ च हे रिशादसा
> हिंसकानां क्षेप्तारौ युवामस्मभ्यं सुम्मं सुखं यंतं । कुरुतमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Since, O Varuṇa and O Mitra, you two come to the most delightful sacrificial ground, therefore you, who are supporters of men and hurlers away of the harmful, bring us happiness; that is, you grant it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- हिरण्ययं (*hiraṇyayaṃ*)—pleasing and attractive
- योनिं (*yoniṃ*)—to the place of sacrifice
- आ सदथः (*ā sadathaḥ*)—you come (then)
- चर्षणीनां (*carṣaṇīnāṃ*)—of men
- धर्तारा (*dhartārā*)—supporters
- रिशादसा (*riśādasā*)—destroyers of foes
- सुम्मं (*summaṃ*)—happiness
- यंतं (*yaṃtaṃ*)—you bring about

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, when you come to the pleasing and attractive place of sacrifice, then you, who are supporters of men and destroyers of foes, bring happiness to all of us.

##### English translation
When you come, Varuna and Mitra, to the delightful place of sacrifice, then, supporters of men, destroyers of foes, you bring felicity.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्वे॒ हि वि॒श्ववे॑दसो॒ वरु॑णो मि॒त्रो अ॑र्य॒मा ।  
> व्र॒ता प॒देव॑ सच्चिरे॒ पांति॒ मर्त्यं॑ रि॒षः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वे । हि । विश्ववेदसः । वरुणः । मित्रः । अर्यमा ।  
> व्रता । पदाइव । सश्चिरे । पांति । मर्त्यं । रिषः ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 52

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> विश्वे हि सर्वॆऽपि । हीति चार्थे । विश्ववेदसः सर्वविदः सर्वधना वा वरुणो
> मित्रोऽर्यमा चैते त्रयः सर्वे व्रतास्मदीयानि कर्माणि पदेव पदानीव
> स्थानानीव सश्चिरे । संगता भवंति । आगत्य च मां मर्त्यं रिषो हिंसकात्पांति । रक्षंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

All of them, "hi" being here in the sense of "and": the omniscient or all-wealthy Varuṇa, Mitra and Aryaman, these three, all of them, join together at our rites as if at their own places. Having come, they protect me, a mortal, from the harmful.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विश्ववेदसः (*viśvavedasaḥ*)—the omniscient
- वरुणः मित्रः अर‍्यमा च (*varuṇaḥ mitraḥ aryamā ca*)—known as Varuṇa, Mitra and Aryaman
- विश्वे हि (*viśve hi*)—all the deities also
- व्रता (*vratā*)—our places of rites
- पदा इव (*padā iva*)—regarding them as if they were their own places
- सच्चिरे (*saccire*)—they gather together
- मर्त्यं (*martyaṃ*)—the man who is the sacrificer
- रिषः (*riṣaḥ*)—from the harmful one
- पांति (*pāṃti*)—they protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

All the deities, the omniscient ones known as Varuṇa, Mitra and Aryaman, regard our place of sacrifice as if it were their own place, come and gather together, and protect the man who is the sacrificer from the harmful one.

##### English translation
Mitra, Varuna, Aryaman, who are possessed of omniscience, are all associated at our rites, as if in (their respective) stations, and protect the worshipper from the malignant.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ते हि स॒त्या ऋ॑त॒स्पृश॑ ऋ॒तावा॑नो॒ जने॑जने ।  
> सु॒नी॒थासः॑ सु॒दान॑वो॒ऽंहोश्चि॑दुरु॒चक्र॑यः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ते । हि । सत्याः । ऋत्स्पृशः । ऋत्वानः । जनेजने ।  
> सुनीथासः । सुदानवः । अंधोः । चित् । उरुचक्रयः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 53

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते हि खलु सत्याः सत्यभूताः सत्यफला वा ऋतस्पृश उदकस्पर्शत्वस्य कर्तार
> ऋतावानो यज्ञवंतो जने जने सर्वॆषु यजमानेषु सुनीथासः सुनयनाः
> सुदानवः सुदानाः । हीति प्रसिद्धवाची सर्वत्र संबंधनीयः । एवं महानुभावा
> वरुणादयोऽ०होश्चित् । चिदप्यर्थे । पापिनोऽपि स्वस्तोतुरुरुचक्रयः प्रभूतधनादिकर्तारः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

They indeed are true, that is, of true being or of true fruit; ṛtaspṛśaḥ, the causers of contact with water; ṛtāvānaḥ, possessed of sacrifice; among each and every sacrificer they are good guides and good givers. The word "hi" signifies "well known" and is to be connected throughout. Such greatly powerful ones, Varuṇa and the others, are doers of abundant wealth and the like even for a sinner who is their praiser, "cit" being in the sense of "even".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते हि (*te hi*)—these very deities
- सत्याः (*satyāḥ*)—being true in nature
- ऋतस्पृशः (*ṛtaspṛśaḥ*)—being distributors of water
- ऋतावानः (*ṛtāvānaḥ*)—being protectors of sacrifice
- जने जने (*jane jane*)—to every human person
- सुनीथासः (*sunīthāsaḥ*)—being excellent guides
- सुदानवः (*sudānavaḥ*)—being excellent givers
- अंहोश्चित् (*aṃhościt*)—even if he is a doer of sinful deeds (if he is a sacrificer)
- उरुचक्रयः (*urucakrayaḥ*)—they are also those who raise him up and benefit him abundantly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

These very deities, being true in nature, distributors of water, protectors of sacrifice, excellent guides and excellent givers to every human person, are also those who raise up and abundantly benefit even a doer of sinful deeds, if he is a sacrificer.

##### English translation
They verily are observers of truth, distributors of water, protectors of holy rites amongst men : guides in the right way, liberal donors : and bencfactors even of the sinner (who worships them).
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> को नु वां॑ मि॒त्रास्तु॑तो॒ वरु॑णो वा त॒नूनां॑ ।  
> तत्सु वा॒मेष॑ते म॒तिरत्रि॑भ्य॒ एष॑ते म॒तिः ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 54

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कः । नु । वां । मित्र । अस्तुतः । वरुणः । वा । तनूनां ।  
> तत् । सु । वां । आ । ईषते । मतिः । अत्रिभ्यः । आ । ईषते । मतिः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्र वां युवयोर्मध्यॆ त्वं च वरुणो वा को नु अस्तुतः ।
> सर्वैरस्तूयमानः कोऽस्ति । उभावपि स्तुत्यावित्यर्थः । तनूनामिति तृतीयार्थे
> षष्ठी । तनुभिः स्तुतिभिरस्तुत इति संबंधः ॥ अत्र मित्रशब्देन मित्रस्यैकस्यैव संबोधनं
> वरुणेळास्वंतः । ऋग्वे. ५-६२-६ । इत्यत्रेवात्र द्विवचनसूचकस्याकारस्यानाम्नानात् ।
> लोकेषु बहुषु संनिहितेष्टेकमेव संबोध्य युवयोरिदं युष्माकविदमिति
> प्रयुंजते ॥ तत्तस्मात् यस्मात्सर्वॆ स्तुवंति तस्मात् । तनूनामित्येतद्व्यवहितमप्युत्तरत्र वा योज्यं । तनूनामल्पमतीनामस्माकं मतिः स्तुतिर्वां युवां
> सुष्टु एषते । अभिगच्छति । अत्रिभ्योऽत्रिगोत्रेभ्योऽस्मभ्यमस्मदीयमतिरेषते ।
> आदरार्थं पुनर्वचनं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra, which of you two, you or Varuṇa, is not praised? Who is there who is not praised by all? The meaning is that both are to be praised. "Tanūnām" is a genitive in the sense of the instrumental: he is not praised by praises, this is the connection. Here by the word "mitra" Mitra alone is addressed, as in वरुणेळास्वंतः (*varuṇeḷāsvaṃtaḥ*) (Ṛgve. 5-62-6)**NOTE**, because the letter ā that indicates the dual is not read. In the world, when many are present, one only is addressed and "of you two", "of you" is used. Therefore, because all praise, "tanūnām", though separated, is to be connected with what follows: the praise of us, of little understanding, goes well to you two. The praise of us, of the Atri family, goes to you. The repetition is for the sake of respect.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra
- वां (*vāṃ*)—among you (whether you)
- वा (*vā*)—or
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- कः (*kaḥ*)—who
- तनूनां (*tanūnāṃ*)—by our praises
- अस्तुतः (*astutaḥ*)—has not been praised
- तत् (*tat*)—therefore
- मतिः (*matiḥ*)—our praise
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- सु (*su*)—with devotion
- एषते (*eṣate*)—approaches
- अत्रिभ्यः (*atribhyaḥ*)—of us, born in the Atri family
- मतिः (*matiḥ*)—the praise
- एषते (*eṣate*)—certainly approaches you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra, among you, whether you or Varuṇa, who has not been praised by our praises? Therefore our praise approaches you with devotion. The praise of us, born in the Atri family, certainly approaches you.

##### English translation
Which of you, Mitra and varuna, has not been celebrated in (our) praise, for therefore do our thoughts tend towards you ? the thoughts of the race of Atri tend towards you.
### Printed p. 55
## Sūkta 68 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र वो मित्रायेति पंचर्चं द्वादशं सूक्तमात्रेयस्य यजतस्यार्षं
> गायत्रं मैत्रावरुणं । तथा चानुक्रम्यते । प्र वो गायत्रमिति ॥ अग्निष्टोमे
> प्रातःसवने मैत्रावरुणशस्त्र एतत्सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च । प्र वो मित्राय प्र मित्रयोर्वरुणयोः । आ. ५-१० । इति॥ अभिप्लवषडहे पृष्ठ षडहे च स्तोमनिमित्तावापार्थमिदमादीनि द्वितीयवर्जितानि चत्वारि सूक्तानि । तथैव सूत्रितं । प्र वो
> मित्रायेति चतुर्णां द्वितीयमुद्धरेत् । आ. ७-५ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning "pra vo mitrāya", of five Ṛks, is the twelfth. Its seer is Yajata of the Atri family, its metre is Gāyatrī, and its deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; the Anukramaṇikā says so. In the Agniṣṭoma, at the morning pressing, this sūkta is used for the śastra of the Maitrāvaruṇa priest, and this is set out in the sūtra (Ā. Sū. 5-10). In the Abhiplava-ṣaḍaha and the Pṛṣṭha-ṣaḍaha, for the insertion of stomas, this sūkta and the next three, omitting the second, are used; so also the sūtra says (Ā. Sū. 7-5).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning "pra vo mitrāya" is the twelfth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has five Ṛks. For this sūkta the seer is the one called Yajata Ātreya; the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "pra vo gāyatram". In the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice, at the time of the morning pressing, this sūkta is applied to the śastra mantras recited by the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa, as set out by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra (Ā. Sū. 5-10). And in the sacrifices called Abhiplava-ṣaḍaha and Pṛṣṭha-ṣaḍaha, for the stomāvāpa (for increasing the stoma), the application of the four sūktas beginning with this one, leaving out the second, is set out by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra (Ā. Sū. 7-5).

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 68
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 68
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 6
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5
- Seer: Yajata Ātreya
- Deity: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Metre: Gāyatrī
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र वो॑ मि॒त्राय॑ गायत॒ वरु॑णाय वि॒पा गि॒रा ।  
> महि॑क्षत्रावृ॒तं बृ॒हत् ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 56

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । वः । मित्राय । गायत । वरुणाय । विपा । गिरा ।  
> महिक्षत्रौ । ऋतं । बृहत् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मदीया ऋत्विजो वो । यूयमित्यर्थ्यः । मित्राय वरुणाय च विपा
> व्याप्तया गिरा स्तुत्या प्र गायत । स्तुतिं कुरुत । स्तुत्या स्तुवतेत्येतत्पाकं
> पचतीतिवत् । हे महिक्षत् प्रभूतबलौ युवामृतं यज्ञं बृहन्महदतिप्रशस्तं ।
> स्तुत्यर्थवागच्छतमिति शेषः । अथवा महत् प्रभूतमृतं स्तोत्रं शृणुतमिति शेषः॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O my priests, "vaḥ" means you: sing forth to Mitra and to Varuṇa with a pervading speech, that is, praise them (as in "he cooks a cooking": praising with praise). O you two of abundant strength, come to the great, exceedingly praised sacrifice (ṛta); "come for the sake of the praise" is to be supplied. Or else: "listen to the great, abundant praise (ṛta)" is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O priests)
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- मित्राय (*mitrāya*)—Mitra
- वरुणाय (*varuṇāya*)—and Varuṇa, addressing them
- विपा (*vipā*)—with pervading
- गिरा (*girā*)—praise
- प्र गायत (*pra gāyata*)—praise and extol exceedingly
- महिक्षत्रा (*mahikṣatrā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa, of exceeding strength
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—great
- ऋतं (*ṛtaṃ*)—to the sacrifice (be pleased to come)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, address Mitra and Varuṇa and extol them exceedingly with pervading praise. O Mitra and Varuṇa, of exceeding strength, be pleased to come to the great sacrifice.

##### English translation
Sing loud with lusty praise to Mitra and to Varuna ; (come), mighty deities, to the great sacrifice.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒म्राजा॒ या स्फु॒तियो॑नी मि॒त्रश्चो॒भा वरु॑णश्च ।  
> दे॒वा दे॒वेषु॑ प्रश॒स्ता ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 57

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> संराजा । या । धृतयोनी इति धृतयोनी । मित्रः । च । उभा । वरुणः । च ।  
> देवा । देवेषु । प्रशस्ता ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या यौ मित्रश्च वरुणश्च । परस्परापेक्षया चशब्दः । उभोभौ सम्राजा
> सर्वस्य स्वामिन् घृतयोनी उदकस्योत्पादक् देवा द्योतमानौ देवेषु मध्ये
> प्रशस्ता प्रकर्षेण स्तुत्यौ । तौ गायतेति पूर्वत्रान्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those two, Mitra and Varuṇa (the word "ca" is used in mutual dependence), both are sovereign, that is, lords of all; originators of water; shining; eminent among the gods, that is, exceedingly worthy of praise. The sentence connects with the earlier verse: sing to them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- या (*yā*)—which
- मित्रश्च (*mitraśca*)—Mitra
- वरुणश्च (*varuṇaśca*)—and Varuṇa
- उभा (*ubhā*)—both of them
- समूजा (*samūjā*)—are lords of the whole world
- धृतयोनी (*dhṛtayonī*)—are originators of water
- देवा (*devā*)—being shining
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—among the gods
- प्रशस्ता (*praśastā*)—are worthy of the highest praise (praise such Mitra and Varuṇa, addressing them)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Praise, addressing them, those Mitra and Varuṇa who are both lords of the whole world, originators of water, and, being shining, worthy of the highest praise among the gods.

##### English translation
The Mitra and Varuna, who are both sovereign rulers, originators of the rain, eminent deties among the gods.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता नः॑ शक्तं॒ पार्थि॑वस्य म॒हो रा॒यो दि॒व्यस्य॑ ।  
> महि॑ वां क्ष॒त्रं दे॒वेषु॑ ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 58

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । नः । शक्तं । पार्थिवस्य । महः । रायः । दिव्यस्य ।  
> महि । वां । क्षत्रं । देवेषु ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता तौ देवौ नोऽस्मदर्थं पार्थिवस्य पृथिवीसंबद्धस्य दिव्यस्य दिवि भवस्य च
> महो महतो रायो धनस्य शक्तं । समर्थौ भवतं । दातुमिति शेषः । हे देवौ
> वां युवयोर्महि महत्पूज्यं क्षत्रं बलं देवेषु प्रसिद्धं स्तुम इति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those two gods, for our sake, be able, that is, capable, to give great wealth, both that connected with the earth and that which is in heaven ("to give" is to be supplied). O two gods, your great, adorable might is well known among the gods; "we praise it" is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ता (*tā*)—those same Mitra and Varuṇa
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us, for giving
- पार्थिवस्य (*pārthivasya*)—connected with the earth
- दिव्यस्य (*divyasya*)—connected with the heavenly world
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—and exceedingly great
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—wealth
- शक्तं (*śaktaṃ*)—they are capable of
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- महि (*mahi*)—great
- क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—might
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—is to be praised among the gods

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those same Mitra and Varuṇa are capable of the wealth, connected with the earth, connected with the heavenly world and exceedingly great, that is to be given to us (they are its lords). Your great might is to be praised among the gods.

##### English translation
They two are able (to grant us) of great terrestrial and celestial riches: great is your might among the gods.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒तमृ॒तेन॒ सपं॑तेषि॒रं दक्ष॑माशाते ।  
> अ॒द्रुहा॑ दे॒वौ व॑र्धेते ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 59

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यतं । यतेन । सपंता । इषिरं । दक्षं । आशाते इति ।  
> अद्रुहा । देव् । वर्धेते इति ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतेनोदकेन निमित्तेन ऋतं यज्ञं संपंता स्पृशंताविषिरमेषणवंतं
> दक्षं प्रवृद्धं यजमानं हविर्वाशाते । व्याप्नुतः । अद्रुहाद्रोग्धारौ देवौ
> मित्रावरुणौ युवां वर्धेते । प्रवृद्धौ भवतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

By water as the cause, touching the sacrifice, the two pervade the sacrificer who is eager, strong and grown, or his oblation. O gods Mitra and Varuṇa, who do no harm, you two grow; you become greatly grown.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यतेन (*yatena*)—with water in the form of rain
- यतं (*yataṃ*)—the sacrifice
- सपंता (*sapaṃtā*)—making fruitful
- इषिरं (*iṣiraṃ*)—skilled in rites
- दक्षं (*dakṣaṃ*)—and strong, the sacrificer
- आशाते (*āśāte*)—they favour
- अद्रुहा (*adruhā*)—who do no harm (to the performer of the sacrifice)
- देवौ (*devau*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- वर्धेते (*vardhete*)—may you prosper

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Making the sacrifice fruitful with water in the form of rain, Mitra and Varuṇa favour the sacrificer who is skilled in rites and strong. O Mitra and Varuṇa, who do no harm to the performer of the sacrifice, may you prosper.

##### English translation
Rewarding with rain the holy rite, they favour the zealous worshipper : benevolent deities, may you prosper.
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वृ॒ष्टिद्या॑वा री॒त्या॑पे॒षस्पती॒ दानु॑मत्याः ।  
> बृ॒हंतं॒ गर्त॑माशाते ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 60

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वृष्टिद्यावा । रीतिआपा । इषः । पती इति । दानुमत्याः ।  
> बृहंतं । गर्तं । आशाते इति ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वृष्टिद्यावा । वृष्टर्था द्यौः स्तुतिर्ययोस्तौ वृष्टिद्यावा । अथवा वृष्टिर्वर्षिका द्यौरंतरिक्षं याभ्यां तौ तादृशौ । रीत्यापा ॥ री गतिरेषणयोः ॥ रीतिः
> प्राप्तिः । सैवापास्ति रभिमतप्राप्तिर्ययोस्तौ तादृशौ । इषोऽन्नस्य पती स्वामिन् ।
> वृष्टिप्रदत्वात् स्वामित्वं । दानुमत्या दानवत्याः । दातृषूचिताया इत्यर्थः ।
> एतदिड्विशेषणं । एवं महानुभावौ बृहंतं महांतं गर्तं रथमाशाते । व्याप्पुतः ।
> अधितिष्ठतो यागार्थं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Vṛṣṭidyāvā": those whose praise (dyauḥ) is for the sake of rain; or else those by whom rain, the rainy season, and the sky are brought about. "Rītyāpā": rī is in the sense of going and seeking; rīti is attainment; those for whom there is the attainment of what is desired.**NOTE** They are the lords of food; they are lords because they give rain. "Dānumatyā" means possessed of giving, that is, suited to givers; this is an adjective of food (iṣ). Such greatly powerful ones mount the great "garta", that is, the chariot, pervading it, for the sake of the sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृष्टिद्यावा (*vṛṣṭidyāvā*)—those who send down rain from the atmosphere
- रीत्यापा (*rītyāpā*)—those who cause the desired object to be obtained
- दानुमत्याः (*dānumatyāḥ*)—fit for giving
- इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—of food
- पती (*patī*)—and lords, Mitra and Varuṇa
- बृहंतं (*bṛhaṃtaṃ*)—great
- गर्तं (*gartaṃ*)—chariot
- आशाते (*āśāte*)—they mount

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Mitra and Varuṇa, who send down rain from the atmosphere, who cause the desired object to be obtained, and who are lords of the food that is fit for giving, mount the great chariot.

##### English translation
Senders of rain from heaven, granters of desires, lords of sustenance suited to the liberal donors (of oblations), they ascend their spacious car.
### Printed p. 61
## Sūkta 69 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> श्री रोचनेति चतुर्यचं त्रयोदशं सूक्तं । उरुचक्रिर्नामात्रेय
> ऋषिः । त्रिष्टुप् छंदः । मित्रावरुणौ देवता । अनुक्रम्यते च । श्री रोचना
> चतुष्कमुरुचक्रिरिति ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning "śrī rocanā", of four Ṛks, is the thirteenth. The seer is the one called Urucakri of the Atri family, the metre is Triṣṭubh, the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa. The Anukramaṇikā also says so. The application is by indication (laiṅgika).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning "śrī rocanā" is the thirteenth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. For this sūkta the seer is the one called Urucakri Ātreya; the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "śrī rocanā catuṣkam urucakriḥ". The application is by indication.

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 69
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 69
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 7
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 4
- Seer: Urucakri Ātreya
- Deity: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Metre: Triṣṭubh
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श्री रो॑च॒ना व॑रुण॒ त्रेरु॒त द्यून्रीणि॑ मित्र धारयथो॒  ॑
> रजांसि ।  
> वा॒वृ॒धा॒नाव॒मतिं॑ क्ष॒त्रिय॒स्यानु॑ व्र॒तं रक्ष॑माणावजु॒र्य० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> श्री । रोचना । वरुण । त्रीन् । उत । द्यून् । त्रीणि । मित्र । धारयथः । रजांसि ।  
> ववृधान् । अमतिं । क्षत्रियस्य । अनु । व्रतं । रक्षमाण् । अजुर्य० ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 62

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वरुण हे मित्र युवां त्री त्रीणि रोचना रोचनानि द्युलोकान्
> धारयथ इति संबंधः । उतापि च त्रीन्दून् द्योतमानानंतरिक्षलोकान्दारयथः ।
> तथा त्रीणि रजांसि भूलोकान् । कीदृशौ युवां । वावृधान् वर्धमान् क्षत्रियस्य ।
> क्षत्रं बलं । तद्वत इंद्रस्य । यद्वा । क्षत्रियजातीयस्य यजमानस्यामतिं ।
> रूपनामैतत् । रूपं व्रतं कर्म चाजुर्यमजीर्णमविरतं वा रक्षमाण् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Varuṇa, O Mitra, you uphold three luminous regions, the heavenly worlds; and also three shining regions, the atmospheric worlds; and likewise the three "rajas", the earthly worlds. Of what sort are you? Increasing the "amati", the form, of the kṣatriya, kṣatra being strength, that is, of Indra who possesses it; or else, of the sacrificer of the kṣatriya class. "Amati" is a word for form. And you protect the rite (vrata), unfailing, that is, undecaying or uninterrupted.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra (both of you)
- क्षत्रियस्य (*kṣatriyasya*)—of the mighty Indra
- अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)—the form
- वावृधानौ (*vāvṛdhānau*)—causing to increase
- आजुर्यं (*ājuryaṃ*)—undecaying
- व्रतं (*vrataṃ*)—rite
- अनुरक्षमाणौ (*anurakṣamāṇau*)—continually protecting
- श्री रोचना (*śrī rocanā*)—the three heavenly worlds full of splendour
- त्रीन् द्यून् (*trīn dyūn*)—the three shining atmospheres
- उत (*uta*)—and
- त्रीणि रजांसि (*trīṇi rajāṃsi*)—the three earthly worlds
- धारयथः (*dhārayathaḥ*)—you uphold

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, both of you, causing the form of the mighty Indra to increase, and continually protecting the undecaying rite, you uphold the three heavenly worlds full of splendour, the three shining atmospheres and the three earthly worlds.

##### English translation
Mitra and Varuna, you uphold the three realms of light, the three heavens; the three regions (of the earth), augmenting the force of the vigorous (Indra), and protecting the imperishable rite.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इरा॑वतीर्वरुण धे॒नवो॑ वां॒ मधु॑मद्वां॒ सिंध॑वो मित्रदुहे ।  
> त्रय॑स्तस्थुवृष॒भास॑स्ति सृ॒णां धि॒षणा॑नां रेतो॒धा विद्यु॒मंतः॑॥

### Printed p. 63

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इरावतीः । वरुण । धेनवः । वां । मधुमत् । वां । सिंधवः । मित्र । दुह्रे ।  
> त्रयः । तस्थुः । वृषभासः । तिसृणां । धिषणानां । रेतःधाः । वि । द्युमंतः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वरुण हे मित्र नां युवरोराज्ञया धेनवो गाव इरावतीः । इरा
> क्षीरलक्षणा । तद्वत्यो भवंति । तथा सिंधवः स्यंदनशीला मेघा नद्यो वा
> मधुमन्मधुररसमुदकं द्रुहे । दुहंति । तथा त्रयस्त्रिसंख्याका वृषभासो
> वर्षितारो रेतोधा उदकस्य धारका द्युमंतो दीप्तिमंतोऽग्नि वाय्वादित्यास्तिसृणां त्रिसंख्याकानां धिषणानां स्थानानां पृथिव्यंतरिक्षद्युलोकानां स्वामिनः
> संतो वि विविधं प्रत्येकं तस्थुः । तिष्ठंति । युवयोरनुग्रहात् त्रयोऽपि
> देवास्त्रिषु स्थानेषु वर्तंत इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Addressing Varuṇa and Mitra, Sāyaṇa says that by your command the cows (dhenu) are full of milk (irā means milk), and likewise the flowing ones, that is the clouds or the rivers, yield sweet water. In the same way three shining ones who send rain and hold the water, namely Agni, Vāyu and Āditya, have become the lords of the three places (the earth, the atmosphere and the heaven), and stand there each separately. The meaning is that by your favour all three deities abide in the three places.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your (command)
- धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*)—the cows
- इरावतीः (*irāvatīḥ*)—are full of milk
- सिंधवः (*siṃdhavaḥ*)—the rivers
- मधुमत् (*madhumat*)—sweet water
- दुह्रे (*duhre*)—yield
- वृषभासः (*vṛṣabhāsaḥ*)—the rain-givers
- रेतोधाः (*retodhāḥ*)—the holders of water
- द्युमंतः (*dyumaṃtaḥ*)—the radiant ones
- त्रयः (*trayaḥ*)—the three, Agni, Vāyu and Āditya
- तिस्कृणां धिषणानां (*tiskṛṇāṃ dhiṣaṇānāṃ*)—of the three places, the earth, the atmosphere and the heaven (as lords)
- वितस्थुः (*vitasthuḥ*)—stand established each separately

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, by your command the cows are full of milk. By your command the rivers yield sweet water. By that same command of yours, the rain-givers, holders of water and radiant ones, Agni, Vāyu and Āditya, stand each separately established as lords of the three places, the earth, the atmosphere and the heaven.

##### English translation
Mitra and Varuna, the cows are full of milk through your (command) and the rivers yield through your (will) sweet water; through you the three radiant receptacles and showers of rain stand severally in their threes pheres.
### Printed p. 64
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रा॒तर्दे॒वीमदि॑तिं जोहवीमि म॒ध्यंदि॑न॒ उदि॑ता॒ सूर्य॑स्य ।  
> रा॒ये मि॑त्रावरुणा स॒र्वता॒तेळे॑ तो॒काय॒ तन॑याय॒ शं  
> योः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रातः । देवीं । अदितिं । जोहवीमि । मध्यंदिने । उत् इता । सूर्यस्य ।  
> राये । मित्रावरुणा । सर्वताता । ईळे । शोकाय । तनयाय । शं । योः ॥ ३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहमृषिः प्रातः प्रातःकालेऽदितिमखंडनीयां देवानां जननीं देवीं
> द्योतमानां जोहवीमि । । आह्वयामि । तथा मध्यंदिने सूर्यस्योदितोदितौ
> तत्समृद्धिकाले माध्यंदिने सवने जोहवीमि । हे मित्रावरुणा युवां राये
> धनप्राप्तॆये सर्वतात् यज्ञ ईळे । स्वामि । पुनः किमर्थं । तोकाय पुत्राय
> तनयाय तत्पुत्राय च शमरिष्टशमनाय योः सुखस्य मिश्रणाय चेळ इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The seer says: I call upon the goddess Aditi, the undivided one, mother of the gods, the shining one, in the morning. Likewise at midday, when the sun has risen and is at the height of its power, at the midday pressing, I call. O Mitra and Varuṇa, I praise you both at the sacrifice at all times, for the attainment of wealth. And for what further purpose? For a son, for his son, for the removal of harm and for the mingling of happiness.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवीं (*devīṃ*)—the divine one
- अदितिं (*aditiṃ*)—Aditi, mother of the gods
- प्रातः (*prātaḥ*)—at the time of the morning pressing
- जोहवीमि (*johavīmi*)—I call with prayer
- सूर्यस्य (*sūryasya*)—of the sun
- उदिता (*uditā*)—when it has risen
- मध्यंदिने (*madhyaṃdine*)—at the midday pressing also I call
- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- सर्वताता (*sarvatātā*)—at all times of the sacrifice
- राये (*rāye*)—for wealth
- तोकाय (*tokāya*)—for a son
- तनयाय (*tanayāya*)—for a son's son (a grandson)
- शंयोः (*śaṃyoḥ*)—for the attainment of happiness and the removal of sorrow
- ईळे (*īḷe*)—I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I call with prayer upon Aditi, divine and mother of the gods, at the time of the morning pressing, and when the sun has risen, at the time of the midday pressing. O Mitra and Varuṇa, I call upon you with prayer at all times of the sacrifice, for wealth, for a son, for a son's son, and for the attainment of happiness and removal of sorrow.
### Printed p. 65

##### English translation
I invoke the divine and bright Aditi at dawn, and at mid-day, when the sun is high : I worship you, Mitra and Varuna, at all seasons, for the sake of riches, for sons and grandsons, for prosperity and happiness.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या ध॒र्तारा॒ रज॑सो रोच॒नस्यो॒तादि॒त्या दि॒व्या पार्थि॑वस्य ।  
> न वां॑ दे॒वा अ॒मृता॒ आ मि॑नंति व्र॒तानि॑ मित्रावरुणा ध्रु॒वाणि॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> या । धर्तारा । रजसः । रोचनस्य । उत । आदित्या । दिव्या । पार्थिवस्य ।  
> न । वां । देवाः । अमृताः । आ । मिनंति । व्रतानि । मित्रावरुणा । ध्रुवाणि ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या यौ रोचनस्य रोचमानस्य रजसो लोकस्य । स्वर्गाख्यस्येत्यर्थः ।
> उतापि च पार्थिवस्य रजसो या यौ धर्तारावादित्यादितेः पुत्रौ दिव्या दिवि भवौ तौ
> युवामीळ इति पूर्वत्र संबंधः । हे मित्रावरुणा नां युवयोध्रुवाणि व्रतानि
> कर्माणि देवा अन्य इंद्रादयोऽमृता अमरणधर्माणोऽपि ना मिनंति । न
> हिंसंति । यतो ध्रुवाण्यत इत्यभिप्रायः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The two who are the upholders of the shining world, that is the world called heaven, and also of the earthly world, the sons of Aditi, born in heaven, those two I praise; this is to be connected with the earlier verse. O Mitra and Varuṇa, your firm works are not violated by the other gods, Indra and the rest, though they are immortal. The sense is that they are not harmed, because the works are firm.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- या (*yā*)—who
- रोचनस्य (*rocanasya*)—of the radiant
- रजसः (*rajasaḥ*)—of the heavenly world
- उत (*uta*)—and
- पार्थिवस्य (*pārthivasya*)—of the earthly world
- धर्तारा (*dhartārā*)—the two upholders
- दिव्या (*divyā*)—the divine ones
- अदित्या (*adityā*)—the sons of Aditi, Mitra and Varuṇa
- ईळे (*īḷe*)—I praise
- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- नां (*nāṃ*)—your
- ध्रुवाणि (*dhruvāṇi*)—firm
- व्रतानि (*vratāni*)—works
- अमृताः (*amṛtāḥ*)—the immortal
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—gods, Indra and the others
- न आ मिनंति (*na ā minaṃti*)—do not harm
### Printed p. 66

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I praise Mitra and Varuṇa, the divine sons of Aditi, who are the upholders of the radiant heavenly world and of the earthly world. O Mitra and Varuṇa, your firm works are not harmed by the immortal gods, Indra and the rest (cannot be transgressed).**NOTE**

##### English translation
I worship you two, divine Adityas, who are upholders of the celestial and terrestrial worlds : the immortal gods impair not, Mitra and Varuna, your eternal works.
## Sūkta 70 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पुरूरुणेति चतुर्ऋचं चतुर्दशं सूक्तमुरुचक्रेरार्षं गायत्रं मैत्रावरुणं । पुरूरुणा गायत्रं त्वित्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ पृष्ठ्याभिप्लवषडहयोः प्रातःसवन
> आवापार्थत्वेनेदमपि सूक्तं पूर्वसूक्तेन सह प्र वो मित्रायेति चतुर्णां
> द्वितीयमुद्धरेदिति ह्युक्तं ॥ चातुर्विंशिकेऽहनि प्रातःसवने मैत्रावरुणशस्त्र
> आद्यस्तृचः षडहस्तोत्रियसंज्ञकः । सूत्रितं च । पुरूरुणा चिद्भ्यस्ति प्रति वां सूर
> उदिते ॥ आ. ७-२ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta of four ṛcs, beginning with the word *purūruṇā*, is the fourteenth sūkta. Its seer is Urucakri, of the family of Atri; its metre is Gāyatrī; its deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; the Anukramaṇikā says that the metre is Gāyatrī. Its use: at the morning pressing of the Pṛṣṭhya and Abhiplava ṣaḍaha rites, this sūkta is also used, together with the preceding sūkta, for insertion, since it has been said that of the four beginning with "Pra vo mitrāya" the second is to be taken up. On the twenty-fourth day, at the morning pressing, the first tṛca (three ṛcs) is the stotriya of the ṣaḍaha in the śastra of the Maitrāvaruṇa priest. It is also given in the sūtra of Āśvalāyana (Ā. 7-2).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, "Purūruṇā", is the fourteenth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It contains four ṛcs. Its seer is Urucakri Ātreya; Mitra and Varuṇa are the deity; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Purūruṇā gāyatraṃ tu". In the Pṛṣṭha and Abhiplava ṣaḍaha sacrifices**NOTE** the use of this sūkta together with the preceding sūkta, at the time of the morning pressing, for the increase of the stoma, has been stated earlier by the sūtra "Pra vo mitrāyeti caturṇāṃ dvitīyam uddharet", and for the Maitrāvaruṇa priest's śastra mantras at the morning pressing on the twenty-fourth day of the sacrifice, the first tṛca (three ṛcs) of this sūkta is to be used as the mantra named the ṣaḍaha-stotriya, as explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, "Purūruṇā ... prati vāṃ sūra udite" (Ā. 7-2).
### Printed p. 67

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 20**NOTE**
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 25; Sūkta 60**NOTE**
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 8
- Number of ṛcs in the sūkta: 4
- Seer: Urucakri Ātreya
- Deity: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Metre: Gāyatrī
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पु॒रू॒रुणा॑ चि॒द्दस्यवो॑ नू॒नं वां॑ वरुण ।  
> मित्र॒ वंसि॑ वां सुम॒तिं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पुरुउरुणा । चित् । हि । अस्ति । अवः । नूनं । वां । वरुण ।  
> मित्र । वंसि । वां । सुमतिं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुण् वां युवयोः पुरूरुणा चित् ॥ अत्र सोः सुपां
> सुलुगित्याकारः ॥ पुरोरपि बहु उरु बहुतरं । अथवा पुरु च तदुरु च पुरूरु ।
> अत्यंतमुरुतरमित्यर्थः । तादृगवो रक्षणं नूनं निश्चयेनास्ति । हि प्रसिद्धौ । चिदिति
> पूरणः । हे वरुण हे मित्र वां युवयोः सुमतिमनुग्रहबुद्धिं वंसि। संभजेय।
> यस्मादवोऽस्ति तस्माद्वंसीति भावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, your protection is surely great, even very great: *puru* and *uru* both mean "much", and the compound means exceedingly large. The word *cit* is a mere filler, and *hi* denotes what is well known. O Varuṇa, O Mitra, may I enjoy your favour, your gracious mind. The sense is that since your protection exists, therefore may I enjoy it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- अवः (*avaḥ*)—protection
- पुरूरुणा चित् (*purūruṇā cit*)—in great abundance
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—certainly
- अस्ति हि (*asti hi*)—exists (by whose favour it is there indeed)
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- सुमतिं (*sumatiṃ*)—gracious mind
- वंसि (*vaṃsi*)—may we enjoy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, may we enjoy that gracious mind of yours, through whose favour your protection is exceedingly great and certainly present.
### Printed p. 68

##### English translation
May I, Mitra and Varuna, enjoy your favour, through which there is assuredly protection.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता वां॑ स॒म्यग॑द्रुह्वा॒णेष॑मश्याम॒ धाय॑से ।  
> व॒यं ते रु॑द्रा स्याम ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । वां । सम्यक् । अद्रुह्वाणा । इषं । आश्याम । धायसे ।  
> वयं । ते । रुद्रा । स्याम ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अद्रुह्वाणा हे अद्रोग्नारौ ता तौ प्रसिद्धौ नां युवां सम्यक् स्तुम
> इति शेषः । स्तोतारो वयमिषमन्नं धायसे पानाय भोजनायाश्याम ।
> प्राप्नुयाम। हे रुद्रा दुःखाद्वा वयितार् रुदद्भिद्रवणीयौ ना ते स्तोतारो
> वयं स्याम । भवेम । समृद्धा इति शेषः । युवाभ्यां स्वभूता वा स्याम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O you two who do no harm, the well-known ones, we praise you fully. We the praisers obtain food for drinking and eating, for our sustenance. O Rudras, those who remove sorrow or who make others weep, may we be your praisers, or may we be prosperous, or may we be your own.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्रुह्वाणा (*adruhvāṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa, you who do us good without doing harm
- ता (*tā*)—the well-known
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- सम्यक् (*samyak*)—fully (we praise)
- धायसे (*dhāyase*)—for our sustenance
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- आश्वाम (*āśvāma*)—may we obtain
- रुद्रा (*rudrā*)—O destroyers of sorrow
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- ते (*te*)—your
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, who do us good without doing harm, we praise you fully. May we obtain food, the means of our sustenance. O destroyers of sorrow, may we be your praisers.

##### English translation
Benignant (deities), may we obtain from you, (who are) such (deities), food for our sustenance: May we, Rudras, be yours.

### Printed p. 69
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पा॒तं नो॑ रुद्रा पा॒युभि॑रु॒त त्रा॑येथां सुत्रा॒त्रा ।  
> तु॒र्याम॒ दस्यू॑न्त॒नूभिः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पातं । नः । रुद्रा । पायुभिः । उत । त्रायेथां । सुत्रात्रा ।  
> तुर्याम । दस्यून् । तनूभिः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे रुद्रोक्तरूपौ देवौ युवां नोऽस्मान् पायुभी रक्षणैः पातं ।
> रक्षतं । उतापि च सुत्रात्रा शोभनेन त्राणेन त्रायेथां । पालयेतं । इष्टप्राप्त्यनिष्टपरिहारभेदेन भेदः । स्तोत्रादिवैकल्याच्छत्रोर्वा त्रायेथां अभिमतप्रापणेन
> रक्षतमित्यर्थः । वयं च तनूभिः पुत्रादिभिः सहिताः स्वीयैरंगैर्वा दस्यून् शत्रुन्
> तुर्याम । हिंस्याम तरेनु वा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O two gods of the form just described as Rudras, protect us with your protections. And protect us with good protection; the two verbs differ according to the difference between obtaining what is wished for and warding off what is not wished for. Or else: protect us from an enemy who comes because our praise and the like are deficient, that is, protect us by bringing us what we desire. And may we, together with our bodies, that is with sons and the like, or with our own limbs, overcome or injure the Dasyus, our enemies, or cross over them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रुद्रा (*rudrā*)—O gods who remove sorrow
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- पायुभिः (*pāyubhiḥ*)—with your protections
- पातं (*pātaṃ*)—protect
- उत (*uta*)—and
- सुत्रात्रा (*sutrātrā*)—with your excellent acts of protection
- त्रायेथाः (*trāyethāḥ*)—protect us
- तनूभिः (*tanūbhiḥ*)—we together with our sons and the like
- दस्यून् (*dasyūn*)—the enemies
- तुर्याम (*turyāma*)—may we injure

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods of the form of Rudras (Mitra and Varuṇa), protect us fully with your excellent acts of protection. May we, together with our sons and the like, injure our enemies.

##### English translation
Protect us with your protections: preserve us with kind preservation : may we, with our descendants, overcome the Dasyus.
### Printed p. 70
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मा कस्या॑द्भुतक्रतू य॒क्षं भु॑जेमा त॒नूभिः॑ ।  
> मा शेष॑सा॒ मा तन॑सा ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मा । कस्य । अद्भुतक्रतू इत्यद्भुतक्रतू । यक्षं । भुजेम । तनूभिः ।  
> मा । शेषसा । मा । तनसा ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अद्भुतक्रतू आश्चर्यकर्माणौ वयं कस्यान्यस्य कस्यचन यक्षं पूजितं
> धनं मा भुजेम । मा भुंजीमहि तनूभिरस्मदीयैरवयवैः । युवयोरनुग्रहात्समृद्धा वयमन्ययाचितधनेन शरीरपोषं न कुर्म इत्यर्थः । तथा शेषसापत्येन सहिता मा भुजेम । तनसा पौत्रादिना सहिता मा भुजेम । अस्मत्कुले न केऽपि
> भुंजीमहीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O two of wondrous works, may we not enjoy the honoured wealth of anyone else, with our own bodies, that is our limbs. The sense is that, made prosperous by your favour, we do not nourish our bodies with wealth begged from others. Likewise may we not enjoy it together with offspring, and may we not enjoy it together with grandsons and the like. The sense is that none in our family would enjoy another's wealth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्भुतक्रतू (*adbhutakratū*)—O gods who perform wonderful works
- कस्य (*kasya*)—of another (than you)
- यक्षं (*yakṣaṃ*)—wealth
- तनूभिः (*tanūbhiḥ*)—with our bodies
- मा भुजेम (*mā bhujema*)—may we not enjoy
- शेषसा (*śeṣasā*)—with our sons also
- मा (*mā*)—we shall not enjoy
- तनसा (*tanasā*)—with grandsons and the like also
- मा (*mā*)—we shall not enjoy (other wealth)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods who perform wonderful works, may we not enjoy the wealth of anyone else, other than the wealth obtained by your favour, either for the nourishment of our own bodies or for the maintenance of our sons, grandsons and the like.

##### English translation
Workers of wonderous deeds, let us not depend upon the bounty of any other (than you), either in our persous, or with our sons and grandsons.
### Printed p. 71
## Sūkta 71 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ नो गंतमिति तृजात्मकं पंचदशं सूक्तं बाहुवृक्तस्यात्रेयस्यार्षं
> गायत्रं मैत्रावरुणं । आ नस्त्रचं बाहुवृक्त इत्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ उक्तः सूक्तविनियोगः ॥ अग्निष्टोमे प्रातःसवने मैत्रावरुणशस्त्रेऽयमनुरूपस्तृचः । सूत्रितं
> च । आ नो मित्रावरुणा नो गंतं रिशादसा । आ. ५-१० । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fifteenth sūkta, beginning "Ā no gantam", consists of three ṛcs; its seer is Bāhuvṛkta Ātreya, its metre is Gāyatrī and its deity is Mitra and Varuṇa, as the Anukramaṇikā says. The use of the sūkta has been stated: in the Agniṣṭoma, at the morning pressing, in the śastra of the Maitrāvaruṇa priest, this tṛca is the anurūpa. It is given in the sūtra of Āśvalāyana (Ā. 5-10).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, "Ā no gantam", is the fifteenth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It contains three ṛcs. Its seer is Bāhuvṛkta Ātreya; Mitra and Varuṇa are the deity; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Ā nastṛcaṃ bāhuvṛktaḥ". The use of the sūkta has been stated earlier. In the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice, among the śastra mantras to be recited by the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa at the time of the morning pressing, this sūkta is to be recited as the anurūpa tṛca, as explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, "Ā no mitrāvaruṇā no gantaṃ riśādasā" (Ā. 5-10).

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 71
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 71
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 9
- Number of ṛcs in the sūkta: 3
- Seer: Bāhuvṛkta Ātreya
- Deity: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Metre: Gāyatrī
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नो॑ गंतं रिशादसा॒ वरु॑ण॒ मित्र॑ ब॒र्हणा॑ ।  
> उपे॒मं चारु॑मध्व॒रं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । गंतं । रिशादसा । वरुण । मित्र । बर्हणा ।  
> उप । इमं । चारुं । अध्वरं ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 72

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वरुण हे मित्र हे रिशादसा शत्रूणां प्रेरकौ युवां नोऽस्माकमिमं
> चारुं चरणीयमध्वरमहिंसकं यज्ञमुपा गंतं । उपागच्छतं । कीदृशौ ।
> बर्हणा परिबर्हणौ निबर्हणौ हंतारौ शत्रूणां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Varuṇa, O Mitra, O you two scatterers, impellers of enemies, come to this our lovely sacrifice, one that is to be practised and that does no harm. What kind of gods are you? Destroyers, that is slayers, of enemies.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रिशादसा (*riśādasā*)—you who scatter enemies
- बर्हणा (*barhaṇā*)—and destroy enemies
- वरुण मित्र (*varuṇa mitra*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- चारुं (*cāruṃ*)—attractive
- इमं अध्वरं (*imaṃ adhvaraṃ*)—to this sacrifice
- उप आ गंतं (*upa ā gaṃtaṃ*)—be gracious and come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, who scatter and destroy enemies, be gracious and come to this attractive sacrifice of ours.

##### English translation
Mitra and Varuna, scatterers of foes, destroyers of enemies, come to this our accessible sacrifice.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्व॑स्य॒ हि प्र॑चेतसा॒ वरु॑ण॒ मित्र॒ राज॑थः ।  
> ई॒शा॒ना पि॑प्यतं॒ धियः॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वस्य । हि । प्रचेतसा । वरुण । मित्र । राजथः ।  
> ईशाना । पिप्यतं । धियः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्र हे वरुण प्रचेतसा प्रकृष्टज्ञान् युवां विश्वस्य राजथः । सर्वस्य
> स्वामिन् भवथः । हीति प्रसिद्धौ । हे ईशाना अस्माकमीश्वर् युवां धियोऽस्मदीयानि कर्माणि पिप्यतं । प्याययतं फलैः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra, O Varuṇa, you two of excellent knowledge reign over all, that is you are lords of everything; the word *hi* denotes that this is well known. O you two who are our lords, fill our works with their fruits.
### Printed p. 73

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra
- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- प्रचेतसा (*pracetasā*)—you who have the best knowledge
- विश्वस्य (*viśvasya*)—of the whole world
- राजथः हि (*rājathaḥ hi*)—you are indeed lords
- ईशाना (*īśānā*)—you who are our lords
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—these works of ours
- पिप्यतं (*pipyataṃ*)—fill with fruits

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, you who have the best knowledge are indeed lords of the whole world. You, our lords, fill these works of ours with fruits.

##### English translation
Sagacious Mitra and Varuna, you reign over all; bestow fulness, lords. upon our ancient rites.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उप॑ नः सु॒तमा ग॑तं॒ वरु॑ण॒ मित्र॑ दा॒शुषः॑ ।  
> अ॒स्य सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उप । नः । सुतं । आ । गतं । वरुण । मित्र । दाशुषः ।  
> अस्य । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नोऽस्माकं सुतमभिषुतं सोमं प्रति हे वरुण हे मित्र युवामुपा
> गतं । उपागच्छतं । दाशुषो हविर्दातुर्ममास्य सोमस्य पीतये पानाय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Varuṇa, O Mitra, come to our pressed Soma, that is the Soma that has been squeezed out, for the drinking of this Soma of me, the giver of the oblation.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra (both of you)
- दाशुषः (*dāśuṣaḥ*)—of me, the giver of the oblation (offered by me)
- अस्य सोमस्य (*asya somasya*)—of this Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for drinking
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—the Soma that has been pressed and made ready
- उप आ गतं (*upa ā gataṃ*)—come to us
### Printed p. 74

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, come to us, turning to this Soma of ours, for the drinking of this Soma juice offered by me (or that is being offered).

##### English translation
Come, Mitra and Varuna, to our effused libation, to drink of the soma juice of the offerer.
## Sūkta 72 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ मित्रे वरुण इति तृचात्मकं षोडशं सूक्तं बाहुवृक्तस्यार्षमौष्ठिहं
> मैत्रावरुणं । आ मित्र औष्णिहमित्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ दशरात्रे तृतीयेऽहनि प्रउगशस्त्रेयं मैत्रावरुणस्य तृचः । सूत्रितं च । आ मित्रे वरुणे वयमश्विनावेह
> गच्छतं । आ. ७-१० । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sixteenth sūkta, beginning "Ā mitre varuṇe", consists of three ṛcs; its seer is Bāhuvṛkta, its metre is Auṣṭiha (Uṣṇih) and its deity is Mitra and Varuṇa, as the Anukramaṇikā says. On the third day of the Daśarātra, in the Praüga śastra, this is the tṛca of the Maitrāvaruṇa priest. It is given in the sūtra of Āśvalāyana (Ā. 7-10).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, "Ā mitre varuṇe", is the sixteenth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It contains three ṛcs. Its seer is Bāhuvṛkta Ātreya; Mitra and Varuṇa are the deity; the metre is Uṣṇik. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Ā mitra auṣṭihaṃ". On the third day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra, when the Praluga**NOTE** śastra mantras are recited, this sūkta is used for the tṛca (three ṛcs) recited by the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa, as explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, "Ā mitre varuṇe vayam aśvināv eha gacchatam" (Ā. 7-10).

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 22**NOTE**
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 72
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 1**NOTE**
- Number of ṛcs in the sūkta: 3
- Seer: Bāhuvṛkṣa Ātreya**NOTE**
- Deity: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Metre: Uṣṭik

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ मि॒त्रे वरु॑णे व॒यं गी॒र्भिर्जु॑हुमो अत्रि॒वत् ।  
> नि ब॒र्हिषि॑ सदतं॒ सोम॑पीतये ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । मित्रे । वरुणे । वयं । गीर्भिः । जुहुमः । अत्रिवत् ।  
> नि । बर्हिषि । सदतं । सोमपीतये ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयमात्रेया मित्रे वरुणे मित्रावरुणयोरर्थाय गीर्भिर्मंत्रैरा
> जुहुमः अत्रिवत् अस्मद्द्योत्रप्रवर्तकोऽत्रिरिव । तस्माद्दे मित्रावरुणौ सोमपीतये
> बर्हिषि नि षदतं । निषीदतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

We, the Ātreyas, invoke Mitra and Varuṇa, for the sake of Mitra and Varuṇa, with songs, that is with mantras, like Atri, the originator of our praise. Therefore, O Mitra and Varuṇa, sit down upon the sacred grass to drink the Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we, born in the family of Atri
- अत्रिवत् (*atrivat*)—following the sage Atri himself
- मित्रे (*mitre*)—Mitra
- वरुणे (*varuṇe*)—Varuṇa, addressed
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with mantras (praying)
- आ जुहुमः (*ā juhumaḥ*)—we call
- सोमपीतये (*somapītaye*)—for the drinking of Soma
- बर्हिषि (*barhiṣi*)—on the seat of darbha grass
- नि सदतं (*ni sadataṃ*)—sit down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

We, born in the family of Atri, praise Mitra and Varuṇa as Atri praised them, and with those same mantras we pray to them and call them. O Mitra and Varuṇa, sit down upon the seat of darbha grass to drink the Soma.

##### English translation
We invoke Mitra aud Varuna with hymns, like (our progenitor) Atri; sit down upon the sacred grass to drink the some libation.

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व्र॒तेन॑ स्थो ध्रु॒वक्षे॑मा॒ धर्म॑णा यात॒यज्ज॑ना ।  
> नि ब॒र्हिषि॑ सदतं॒ सोम॑पीतये ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> व्रतेन । स्थः । ध्रुवक्षेमा । धर्मणा । यातयत्जना ।  
> नि । बर्हिषि । सदतं । सोमपीतये ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणौ युवां धर्मणा जगद्वारकेण व्रतेन कर्मणा योगेन
> ध्रुवक्षेमा स्थः । अविचलितस्थान् भवथः । यातयज्जना । यातयंतः कर्मगु
> प्रवर्तयंतो जना ऋत्विजो ययोस्त्या यातयज्जन् । अथवा यातयंतः
> शत्रुन् हिंसंतो जना भृत्या ययोस्त्या । शिष्टं गतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, you are firm in your abode through your sustaining law, that is through the work by which you uphold the world. The word for "those whose people urge them on" is explained thus: those whose men, the priests, set the rites going; or else, those whose men, their servants, strike down enemies. The rest is clear.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यातयज्जना (*yātayajjanā*)—you who have priests and others who urge you on by praise and the like
- धर्मणा (*dharmaṇā*)—by the world-sustaining
- व्रतेन (*vratena*)—by your work
- ध्रुवक्षेमा (*dhruvakṣemā*)—having an unshaken abode
- स्थः (*sthaḥ*)—you are
- सोमपीतये (*somapītaye*)—for the drinking of Soma
- बर्हिषि (*barhiṣi*)—on the seat of darbha grass
- नि सदतं (*ni sadataṃ*)—sit down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, you who have priests and others urging you on by praise and the like, are of unshaken abode through your world-sustaining work. Sit down on the seat of darbha grass to drink the Soma.

##### English translation
Steady are you in your functions, whom men animate by (their) devotion: come and sit down upon the sacred grass to drink the some libation.

### Printed p. 78
## Sūkta 73 (Maṇḍala 5)
**Introduction: a brief account of the Aśvin deities**
In the six sūktas from this sūkta onwards the Aśvin deities are praised; therefore we give here a brief account of them.
The Aśvin deities are two. It is the custom to say that one is named Dasra and the other Nāsatya. Yet these words are often found used in addressing both together. About their origin there is a story in the Purāṇas. Saṃjñā, the wife of the Sun, being displeased with the Sun for some reason, went to the forest and practised austerities in the form of a mare. The Sun learned of it, went in the form of a stallion to the place where she was, and stayed for some time; it is said that the Aśvin deities were then born to them as sons. In the Ṛgveda there are many sūktas about them. These are most solemn and praise their qualities and their heroic deeds. In the Yajurveda it is said, "अश्विन् वै देवानां भिषजौ (*aśvin vai devānāṃ bhiṣajau*)", that is, these Aśvin deities are the physicians of the gods. It is said in the 116th sūkta of this maṇḍala that once Ṛjāśva, who was grazing goats in the forest at the order of his father, gave a hundred goats as food to the Aśvin deities when they came in the form of wolves (vṛka); his father learned of it and in great anger had his son's eyes torn out and made him blind; then Ṛjāśva prayed to the Aśvin deities, and they, pleased with his praise, gave him eyes and showed him grace. Many such heroic and daring deeds of these deities are described in the 116th to 120th sūktas and elsewhere.
In the Ṛgveda, when certain deities are praised, certain specific words are used. When Agni is praised, words such as kavi, hotṛ and gṛhapati are used; of Indra, words such as śatakratu, vṛtraghna, puruhūta and citrabhānu; of the Aśvin deities, words such as nāsatyā, śubhaspatī, narā and purubhujā. Since all the seers have used many such words for these respective deities, it is clear that those words must have had a specific meaning, besides the meanings that appear to us now or are current in usage.
### Printed p. 79
It is now difficult to say in what sense the seers used those words. Even if, following grammar and by an analysis into parts, we devise some reasonable meaning, it is doubtful whether it was the intention of the earlier seers. Though those words are still in use, the meaning they had in earlier use is not the one they have now. Since their meaning in modern culture is different, it cannot be said that the meaning we give by commentary agrees with the views of the earlier seers.
Since the word Aśvinau is a proper name, that is, it denotes definite persons, it is not right to devise an etymological meaning for the word. Yet the interpreters of the Veda have given this word the meaning "अश्वः अस्यास्तीति अश्वी तौ अश्विन् (*aśvaḥ asyāstīti aśvī tau aśvin*)", that is, possessors of horses or those who ride on horses. For this kind of meaning there is no room in the Ṛgveda, because in many ṛcs the Aśvin deities are addressed as sitting on a chariot, and nowhere is it said that they ride seated on a horse. Therefore their view is not reasonable. But since the Aśvin deities were born of the Sun and of Saṃjñā, the Sun's wife, who were in the form of horses, it appears proper to conjecture that the name Aśvinā or Aśvin came to them from that. The two forms Aśvin and Aśvinau are both dual; they are used, according to the context, in the nominative and the accusative.
The introduction ends.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> षष्ठेऽनुवाके पंचदश सूक्तानि । तत्र यदद्य स्थ इति दशर्चं प्रथमं सूक्तमात्रेयस्य पौरनाम्न आर्षमानुष्टुभमाश्विनं । अनुक्रांतं च । यदद्य दश पौर
> अश्विनं तदानुष्टुभं त्विति । तदित्युक्तत्वादिदमादीनि षट् सूक्तान्याश्विनानि । आनुष्टुभं
> त्विति तुशब्दादिदमुत्तरं च द्वे आनुष्टुभे ॥ प्रातरनुवाक आश्विने क्रतावानुष्टुभे
> छंदस्याश्विनशस्त्रे चेदमादिके द्वे सूक्ते । यदद्य स्थ इति सूक्ते । आ. ४-१५ । इति
> सूत्रितं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the sixth anuvāka there are fifteen sūktas. Of them the first, beginning "yad adya stha", has ten ṛcs; its seer is Paura Ātreya, its metre is Anuṣṭubh and its deity is the Aśvins. The Anukramaṇikā says so. Because the word "tat" is used there, this and the following five sūktas are addressed to the Aśvins. Because of the word "tu" in the Anukramaṇikā's phrase, this and the next sūkta are in Anuṣṭubh. The use: at the Prātaranuvāka, for the Anuṣṭubh verses connected with the Aśvin rite, and for the Aśvin śastra, the two sūktas beginning "yad adya stha" are used, as the sūtra of Āśvalāyana states (Ā. 4-15).

##### Anuvāda

In the sixth anuvāka there are fifteen sūktas. Of them, "Yad adya sthaḥ" is the first sūkta. It contains ten ṛcs. Its seer is Paura Ātreya; the Aśvin deities are the deity; the metre is Anuṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Yad adya daśa paura āśvinaṃ tad ānuṣṭubhaṃ tu". Since the word *tat* is used in the Anukramaṇikā, the six sūktas from this one onwards are to be understood as addressed to the Aśvin deities; since it says *anuṣṭubhaṃ tu*, this sūkta and the next one are to be understood as in the Anuṣṭubh metre.
### Printed p. 80
At the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka mantras, this sūkta and the next are used for the Anuṣṭubh ṛcs connected with the Aśvin rite and for the Aśvin śastra mantras, as explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, "Yad adya stha iti sūkte" (Ā. 4-15).

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 73
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 73
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 11, 12
- Number of ṛcs in the sūkta: 10
- Seer: Paura Ātreya
- Deity: the Aśvins
- Metre: Anuṣṭubh
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद॒द्य स्थः प॑रा॒वति॒ यद॑र्वा॒वत्य॑श्विना ।  
> यद्वा॑ पु॒रू पु॑रुभुजा॒ यदं॒तरि॑क्ष॒ आ ग॑तं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । अद्य । स्थः । परावति । यत् । अर्वावति । अश्विना ।  
> यत् । वा । पुरु । पुरुभुजा । यत् । अंतरिक्षे । आ । गतं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुभुजा बहुषु यज्ञेषु भोक्तारावधिकं रक्षितारौ वा हे अश्विना
> यद्यद्यद्यास्मिन् काले परावत्यत्यंतं दूरदेशे द्युलोके स्थो भवथः । यद्वा ।
> अर्वावत्यरणवति गंतुं शक्ये प्रदेशेऽंतिके स्थः । यद्यदि ना अथवा पुरु बहुप्रदेशेषु स्थः । यद्यद्यंतरिक्षे स्थः तस्मात्सर्वस्मादद्या गतं । आगच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, you who enjoy in many sacrifices, or who protect more: if today, at this time, you are far away, that is in the distant heavenly world; or else, if you are near, in a place that can be reached; or if you are in many places; or if you are in the atmosphere: from all of these come today.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुभुजा (*purubhujā*)—you who accept a share in many sacrifices
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- अद्य (*adya*)—at the time of this sacrifice
- यत् (*yat*)—if
- परावति (*parāvati*)—in a very distant place
- स्थः (*sthaḥ*)—you are
- यत् (*yat*)—or
- अर्वावति (*arvāvati*)—in a near place
- यत् वा (*yat vā*)—or if
