> विऊर्ण्ती । दाशुषे । वार्याणि । पुनः । ज्योतिः । युवतिः । पूर्वथा ।  
> अकरित्यकः ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 175

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एषोषाः प्रतीच्यभिमुखा सती दिवो दुहिता नृन् सर्वान् प्राणिनः प्रति
> भद्रा योषेव कल्याणवेषा योषिदिवाप्सो रूपं नि रिणीते । प्रेरयति । किंच
> दाशुषे हविर्दात्रे यजमानाय वार्याणि वरणीयानि धनानि व्यूर्ण्वती प्रयच्छंती युवतिर्नित्ययौवना सर्वत्र मिश्रयंती वा पुनरद्यापि पूर्वथा पूर्वमिव
> ज्योतिस्तेजोऽकः । करोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Uṣas, facing the west, the daughter of heaven, sends forth her form towards all beings, like an auspicious woman, one of fair dress; she sends it out. Further, to the giver of oblations, the sacrificer, she bestows desirable riches; ever young, that is, ever in youth, or else mingling with all things everywhere, she again now makes light as before.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the atmosphere
- दुहिता (*duhitā*)—the daughter
- एषा (*eṣā*)—this goddess Uṣas
- प्रतीची (*pratīcī*)—facing the west
- भद्रा (*bhadrā*)—auspicious
- योषेव (*yoṣeva*)—like a woman
- नृन् (*nṛn*)—to all beings
- अप्पः (*appaḥ*)—form
- नि रिणीते (*ni riṇīte*)—she provides
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—to the sacrificer who gives oblations
- वार्याणि (*vāryāṇi*)—the much desired riches
- व्यूर्ण्वती (*vyūrṇvatī*)—providing
- युवति (*yuvati*)—the ever young Upastu **NOTE**
- पुनः (*punaḥ*)—again
- पूर्वथा (*pūrvathā*)—as before
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—brilliance
- आकः (*ākaḥ*)—she produces

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Uṣas, the daughter of the atmosphere, facing the west, provides form to all beings like an auspicious woman. Providing the much desired riches to the sacrificer who gives oblations, the ever young Upasu again produces brilliance as before.

##### English translation
Ushas, the daughter of heaven, tending to the west puts forth her beauty like a (well-dressed) woman, bestowing precious treasures upon the offerer of adoration : she ever youthful, brings back the light as (she has done) of old.

## Sūkta 81 (Maṇḍala 5)

**Introduction (Piṭhike)**

**A brief introduction to the deity Savitṛ**

In this sūkta and in the next, the deity Savitṛ is praised. We have spoken briefly about the deity Savitṛ before. Even so, we shall explain here some necessary matters.

### Printed p. 176

Eleven entire sūktas, and parts of some other sūktas besides, praise Savitṛ; his name occurs about 170 times. Of the eleven sūktas, only three are in the first and tenth maṇḍalas, and the rest are in the remaining maṇḍalas. Savitṛ is for the most part a golden deity; those who belong to him and his equipment are all described as golden. His eye is of gold (1-35,8); his hands are of gold (1-35-9 and 10); he has a golden tongue (6-71-3). Such description is the distinction of Savitṛ. His hands are of gold (6-71-1 and 5; 7-45-2), broad (2-38-2), or beautiful (3-33-6). His speech is sweet (6-71-4); his tongue is beautiful (3-54 11). In one place only he is said to have iron jaws (6-71-4). His hair is yellow in colour (1-39-1); the hair of Agni and Indra is also of this colour. His garment is tawny (4-53-2). He has a golden chariot with a golden pole (1-35-2 and 5). This chariot, like himself (5-81-2), has many forms (1-53-3). His chariot has two radiant horses, or else a larger number of dusky horses with white hoofs draw the chariot (1-35-2 and 5; 7-45-1).

Savitṛ's splendour is boundless. His golden lustre is immeasurable; this is rarely granted to other deities (3-38 8; 7-38-1). He spreads or pours out this lustre outwards. By it the atmosphere, heaven and earth, the world, the hollows of the earth and the sky, all become bright (1-35-7 and 8; 4-14-2; 4-53-4; 5-81-2). Raising his strong golden arms, which reach to the horizon, he awakens all beings and blesses them (2-38-2; 4-53-3 and 4; 6-71-1 and 5; 7-45-2). Because other deities are compared to Savitṛ in the raising of the arms, this too may be called a personal quality of his. Agni raises his arms like Savitṛ (1-95-7); as Savitṛ stretches out his arm, Uṣas stretches out the light (7-79-2). In the same way Bṛhaspati is prayed to that the words of praise be uttered aloud (1-190-3). On the path that runs downwards and upwards, Savitṛ goes seated in his golden chariot, looking upon all beings (1-35-2 and 3). Before the time of dawn he sets out the chariot of the Aśvin deities (1-36-10). Following the path of the goddess Uṣas, he too shines (5-81-2). He has measured the regions of the earth; he goes to the three regions of the heavenly world; he goes united with the rays of the sun (5-81-3 and 4). In the Ṛgveda the word "sun-ray" occurs only once. It belongs to Savitṛ. Shining with the rays of the sun, Savitṛ of the yellow hair constantly sheds his lustre in the eastern direction (10-139-1).

### Printed p. 177

He goes around the atmosphere, the three skies and the three shining regions of the upper world, three times (4-53-5). The ancient paths that he follows in the sky are pure and easy to walk upon. Here his worshippers seek him, saying "protect" (1-35-11). They pray to him, "Join the life of the dead to the place where the living are" (10-17,4). It is he who gives immortality to the gods and long life to men (4-54-2). It is he also who bestowed immortality on the Ṛbhus, who came to his house by the power of their works (1-110-2 and 3). As the sun is prayed to, so Savitṛ is prayed to for the removal of bad dreams (5 82-4) and for release from sin (4-54-3). He drives away evil forces and sorcerers (1-35-10; 7-38-7). Like some other deities he too is an Asura (4-53-1). He follows certain fixed laws (4-53-4; 10-34-8; 10-139-3). Waters and winds are subject to his laws (2-38-2). He is the guide of the waters; driven by him, they flow far and wide (3-33-6; compare Nirukta 2.26). Other deities follow his lead (5-81-3). No one can resist his resolve and his independence. Indra, Varuṇa, Mitra, Aśvama **NOTE**, Rudra and others also cannot resist (2-58-7 and 9; 5-83-2). The Vasus, Aditi, Varuṇa, Mitra and Aryaman praise his fame (7-38-3 and 4). Like Pūṣan and Sūrya, Savitṛ is the lord of the moving and the unmoving (4-53-6). All things desired by all are under his control. He alone becomes the giver of good through heaven, earth and sky (1-25-3; 2-38-11). Twice he is called, like Agni, "of the house" (गृह्य (*gṛhya*)), and "belonging to the home" (दमूनः (*damūnaḥ*)) (1-123-3; 6-71-4). Like other deities, he too is the support of the sky (4-53-2; 10-149-4). He is the support of the whole world (4-54-4). He has bound the earth with bonds and set it firm, and has made the sky stand in a void without beams or chariots (10-149-1]. The epithet अपांनपात् (*apāṃnapāt*), "born of the water", which is proper only to Agni, is said of Savitṛ at least once (1-22-6; 10-149-2). It appears that in the same way it is said of Savitṛ in **NOTE**. Yāska (Nirukta 11-32), commenting on the mantra (10-149-2) mentioned above, has said that, since Savitṛ is the cause of rain, he is a deity of the middle region, and since the sun in the sky (Āditya) also has the name Savitṛ, Savitṛ must be a deity of this middle world. Since he is the cause of rain, and since Savitṛ's path lies in the atmosphere, or in the middle world (1-35-11), in the Naighaṇṭuka section the name of this deity stands in the list of the deities of heaven and of the middle world. Once Savitṛ is called Prajāpati also

### Printed p. 178

(4-53-2). In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa the view is expressed that Savitṛ and Prajāpati are one and the same deity (Śa. Brā. 12-3-5-1). In the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa (1-6-4-1) it is said that Prajāpati himself became Savitṛ and created beings. He alone is lord of the power of giving life. By his movements (यामभिः (*yāmabhiḥ*)) he becomes Pūṣan (5-82-5). Pūṣan, possessing the life-giving power, like a nourisher, moves about looking upon all beings (10-139-1). In two mantras (3-62-9 and 10) it is said that Savitṛ and Pūṣan are related. In the first, the favour of Pūṣan, who sees all beings, is asked for. In the second, it is prayed that Savitṛ should so inspire that all the mental activities of those who wish to know his vigour and greatness, and of those who worship him, may be directed towards him. This second is the famous Sāvitrī or Gāyatrī mantra. The custom has arisen of reciting it at the beginning of the study of the Veda. Because Savitṛ is the regulator of ordinances, he is called Mitra (5-81-4). Savitṛ is sometimes called "Bhaga" (5-81-1 and 2; 7-38-1 and 6). Or this too may be an epithet applicable to him. The word "Bhaga" (the deity who gives auspicious fruits to men) is often attached to Savitṛ and used as सविता भगः (*savitā bhagaḥ*). In other places a distinction is made between Savitṛ and Mitra, Pūṣan and Bhaga. In many passages the two words Savitṛ and Sūra are used for one deity. Savitṛ, holding his radiance upward, produces light for the whole world; Sūra, shining abundantly, fills heaven, earth and sky with his rays (4-14-2). In the Ṛks 7-63-1, 2 and 4, the epithet प्रसवितृ (*prasavitṛ*), which belongs to Savitṛ, is used for Sūra. In the third mantra of the same sūkta it appears to be said that Savitṛ himself is Sūra. In some other places also (10-158-1 to 4; 1-35-1 to 11; 1 124-1) it is very hard to separate the two. Savitṛ moves between heaven and earth; he removes disease; he sets the sun in motion (1-35-9); in this sentence the difference between Sūrya and Savitṛ is clearly stated. Savitṛ tells the sun that men are free from sin (1-123-3). He mingles with the rays of the sun (5-81-4), or shines here together with the rays of the sun (1 139-1; compare 10-18-3; 1-157-1; 7-35-8 and 10). At sunrise, Savitṛ, joined with Mitra, Aryaman and Bhaga, is prayed to, so that he may revive the worshipper (7-66-4).

In Yāska's view Savitṛ appears after the darkness has been removed (Ni. 12-12). Sāyaṇa (5-8-4) holds that the sun has the name Savitṛ before sunrise, and the name Sūrya from sunrise to sunset. But since it is said that Savitṛ makes beings go to sleep (4-53-6; 7-45-1), for Savitṛ the evening and

### Printed p. 179

the morning must both be related. In one sūkta (2-38) he is praised as the setting sun; moreover, it appears that there is a rule that all the sūktas addressed to Savitṛ are to be used in the evening rites or in the morning oblations. It is he who makes all two-footed and four-footed creatures rest, and afterwards awakens them (6-71-2; compare 4-53-3 and 7-45-1). He unyoks his horses; he makes those who travel take rest; when his order is given, night comes; the weaver folds up his cloth; and the workman stops his half-finished work at that point (2-38-3 and 4). As it is said that Agni is in the east and Soma in the south, it is customary to say that Savitṛ is in the west (Śa. Brā. 3-2-3-18).

This word Savitṛ is formed from the root सू (*sū*). This root is used in various forms only when the deity Savitṛ is praised. Even in contexts where the same meaning is needed, other roots are seen to be used in mantras addressed to other deities. Encouragement, awakening and reviving are the meanings specially intended for this root. Savitṛ has made every moving thing intent on poetry (प्रासावीत् (*prāsāvīt*)) (1-157-1); you alone are able to give life (प्रसवस्य (*prasavasya*)) (5-81-3); Savitṛ granted immortality (असुवत् (*asuvat*)) to you (1-110-3); Savitṛ is ready to awaken us (सवाय (*savāya*)) (2-38-1); from the sky he sends desired things (सोसवीति (*sosavīti*)) three times a day (3-56-6); O Savitṛ, make us free from sin (सुवतात् (*suvatāt*), 4-54-3); by the power of Savitṛ (सवे (*save*)), may all boons be obtained by us, who are footless **NOTE** in regard to Aditi (5-2- **NOTE**; drive away bad dreams (परासर (*parāsara*)), remove calamities, grant what is good (आसुव (*āsuva*)) (5-22-4 and 5); may Savitṛ drive away disease (अपसानिशत् (*apasāniśat*)) (10-100-8); with this same root Savitṛ is prayed to in many places to grant wealth (2-56-6; etc.). Thus it may be said that this root is wholly reserved for Savitṛ. But two or three times it is used also for Sūra (7-63-2 and 4; 10-37-4). It is used with Uṣas in 7-77-1, for Varuṇa in (2-28-9), for the Ādityas in 8-18-1, and for Mitra, Aramya **NOTE** and Savitṛ together in 7-66-4. Because this root is used so often for Savitṛ, Yāska has called Savitṛ (सर्वस्य प्रसविता (*sarvasya prasavitā*), Ni. 10-31) "the one who brings all to life".

In almost all the contexts in which the word Savitṛ occurs it is joined with the word देव (*deva*). In all these places the word देव (*deva*) seems to have no special sense, but only the ordinary meaning of deity. However that may be, in two passages it is an epithet of Tvaṣṭṛ

### Printed p. 180

(देवः त्वष्टा सविता विश्वरूपा (*devaḥ tvaṣṭā savitā viśvarūpā*), 3-55-19; 10-10-5). Seeing these words joined in this way, one gets the idea that Savitṛ and Tvaṣṭṛ are one.

The name Savitṛ may belong to one power of the sun, which produces life in the world and gives the power of movement. But compared with the sun (the solar orb that we see), Savitṛ may be said not to be a deity perceptible to the outer senses. It is known from the Ṛgveda that the sun is the visible orb, a mass of radiance, in this gross sky, and that Savitṛ is the embodied divine power of that sun.

According to some, the life-giving power itself is Savitṛ; the sun and his fixed course and so on are all secondary matters. For a detailed account of Savitṛ see the special topics of the Gāyatrī mantra (Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 17).

(The introduction is concluded.)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> युंजते मन इति पंचर्चं नवमं सूक्तं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका ।
> युंजते पंच श्यावाश्वः सावित्रं तु जागतमिति । श्यावाश्वो नामात्रेय ऋषिः ।
> जगती छंदः । सविता देवता ॥ पृष्टाभिप्लवषडहयोः प्रथमेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे
> सावित्रनिविद्वानमिदं । सूत्रितं च । युंजते मन इहेह व इति चतस्रः । आ. ७-५ ।
> इति ॥ आद्या ग्रावस्तोत्रेऽपि । सूत्रितं च । युंजते मन उत युंजते धिय आ
> तू न इंद्र क्षुमंतं । आ. ५-१२ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is the ninth sūkta, with five Ṛks, beginning युंजते मन (*yuṃjate mana*). On it the Anukramaṇikā says: युंजते पंच श्यावाश्वः सावित्रं तु जागतं (*yuṃjate paṃca śyāvāśvaḥ sāvitraṃ tu jāgataṃ*). The seer is Śyāvāśva, of the Atri family; the metre is Jagatī; the deity is Savitṛ. On the first day of the Pṛṣṭhya and Abhiplava ṣaḍahas this sūkta is the Sāvitra nivid-hymn in the Vaiśvadeva-śastra. It is enjoined also by the sūtra: युंजते मन इहेह व इति चतस्रः (*yuṃjate mana iheha va iti catasraḥ*) (Ā. 7-5) **NOTE**. The first Ṛk is also used in the Grāvastotra; the sūtra says: युंजते मन उत युंजते धिय आ तू न इंद्र क्षुमंतं (*yuṃjate mana uta yuṃjate dhiya ā tū na iṃdra kṣumaṃtaṃ*) (Ā. 5-12).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning युंजते मनः (*yuṃjate manaḥ*) is the ninth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It has five Ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: युंजते पंच श्यावाश्वः सावित्रं तु जागतं (*yuṃjate paṃca śyāvāśvaḥ sāvitraṃ tu jāgataṃ*). The seer of this sūkta is Śyāvāśva Ātreya; the deity is Savitṛ; the metre is Jagatī. When the Vaiśvadeva-śastra mantras are recited on the first day of the sacrifices called Pṛṣṭhya and Abhiplava ṣaḍaha, this sūkta is to be recited as the nivid-hymn relating to Savitṛ; this is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, युंजते मन इहेव व इति चतस्रः (*yuṃjate mana iheva va iti catasraḥ*) (Ā. 7-5). And when the Grāvas (the stones, instruments for pressing Soma) are praised, the first Ṛk of this sūkta is to be recited; this is set out by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, युंजते मन उत युंजते धिय आ तू न इंद्र क्षुमंतं (*yuṃjate mana uta yuṃjate dhiya ā tū na iṃdra kṣumaṃtaṃ*) (Ā. 5-12).

### Printed p. 181

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 81
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 24
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5
- Seer: Śyāvāśva Ātreya **NOTE**
- Deity: Savitṛ
- Metre: Jagatī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> युं॒जते॒ मन॑ उ॒त युं॑जते॒ धियो॒ विप्रा॒ विप्र॑स्य बृह॒तो  ॒॑॑
> विपश्चितः ।  
> वि होत्रा॑ दधे वयुना॒विदेक॒ इन्म॒ही दे॒वस्य॑ सवि॒तुः  ॑
> परिष्टुतिः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युंजते । मनः । उत । युंजते । धियः । विप्राः । विप्रस्य । बृहतः । विपःचितः ।  
> वि । होत्राः । दधे । वयुनवित् । एकः । इत् । मही । देवस्य । सवितुः । परिस्तुतिः॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ॥ कयडाशं ॥
> विप्रा मेधाविन ऋत्विग्यजमाना मनः स्वीयं सर्वॆषु कर्मसु युंजते ।
> योजयंति । सवित्रनुग्रहाय संकल्पं कुर्वंतीत्यर्थः। उतापि च धियः कर्माण्यपि
> युंजते । प्राप्नुवंति । कस्यानुज्ञयेत्कुच्यते । विप्रस मेधाविनो बृहतो
> महतो विपश्चितः स्तुत्यस्य ज्ञानवतो वा सवितुरनुज्ञयेति । सविता वै प सवानामीश
> इति हि श्रुतिः । स एव सविता होत्राः सप्त होत्रकाणामुचिताः क्रिया वयुनाविद् ।
> वयुनमिति प्रज्ञानाम । तत्तदनुष्ठानविषयप्रज्ञावेत्तैक इत् एक एव विदधे ।
> करोति । पृथक् पृथगवधारयति । किंच तस्य सवितुर्देवस्य परिष्टुतिः स्तुतिर्मही
> महत्यतिप्रभूता । स्तुत्यगोचरेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The wise priests and sacrificers apply their own minds to all the rites, that is, they make a resolve for the favour of Savitṛ. Also they apply their actions, that is, they engage in them. By whose leave? By the leave of the wise, great, learned or praiseworthy Savitṛ. The Śruti says that Savitṛ is the lord of impulses **NOTE**. He alone, the Savitṛ, ordains the proper acts of the seven Hotṛs. He is वयुनाविद् (*vayunāvid*): वयुन (*vayuna*) is a name for knowledge; he who knows the knowledge concerning each performance alone ordains and fixes each separately. Further, the praise of that divine Savitṛ is great, very abundant; the meaning is that it is within the range of praise.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

### Printed p. 182

- विप्राः (*viprāḥ*)—the wise priests and sacrificers
- मनः (*manaḥ*)—their minds
- युंजते (*yuṃjate*)—apply to the rites themselves
- उत (*uta*)—and
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—their rites
- विप्रस्य (*viprasya*)—of the wise one
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—of the one of great form
- विपश्चितः (*vipaścitaḥ*)—of the learned Savitṛ
- युंजते (*yuṃjate*)—they apply
- वयुनावित् (*vayunāvit*)—Savitṛ, who knows the form of each rite
- एक इत् (*eka it*)—alone
- होत्राः (*hotrāḥ*)—the seven Hotṛs (or their acts)
- वि दधे (*vi dadhe*)—makes them perform individually
- देवस्य (*devasya*)—of the divine
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—Savitṛ
- परिष्टुतिः (*pariṣṭutiḥ*)—the praise
- मही (*mahī*)—full of greatness

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The wise priests and sacrificers apply their minds to the rites themselves. And they apply their rites to Savitṛ, who is wise, of great form and learned. Savitṛ, who knows the form of each rite, alone makes the seven Hotṛs perform their acts individually. The praise of the divine Savitṛ is full of greatness.

##### English translation
The wise apply their minds: they perform sacred rites for the propitiation of the intelligent, great, adorable Savitri : he alone, knowing their functions, directs the priests: verily, great is the praise of the divine Savitri.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

विप्राः मनः युंजते । मेधाविनः ऋत्विग्यजमानाः स्वीयं मनः सर्वेषु कर्मसु योजयंति । सवित्रमनुग्रहाय संकल्पं कुर्वंतीत्यर्थः । (*viprāḥ manaḥ yuṃjate | medhāvinaḥ ṛtvigyajamānāḥ svīyaṃ manaḥ sarveṣu karmasu yojayaṃti | savitramanugrahāya saṃkalpaṃ kurvaṃtītyarthaḥ |*). "The wise" means the intelligent priests and the sacrificer: they join their minds in all the rites connected with the sacrifice. That is, they perform all the activities with a willing mind, so that the god Savitṛ may be pleased; this is the meaning.

विप्रस्य बृहतः विपश्चितः—मेधाविनः महतः स्तुत्यस्य ज्ञानवतो वा सवितुः अनुज्ञयेति । (*viprasya bṛhataḥ vipaścitaḥ—medhāvinaḥ mahataḥ stutyasya jñānavato vā savituḥ anujñayeti |*). This god Savitṛ is very intelligent, wise, one who knows the form of all rites, one who knows well the rites of the seven Hotṛs. All the rites that these seven Hotṛs and the other priests and sacrificers have to perform, this god Savitṛ alone can perform.

### Printed p. 183

His power is extraordinary; therefore the sentence of the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa, सविता वै प्रसवानामिशा (*savitā vai prasavānāmiśā*) (Ai. Brā. 7-16), supports this.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्वा॑ रू॒पाणि॒ प्रति॑ मुंचते क॒विः प्रासा॑वीद्भ॒द्रं द्वि॒पदे॒  ॑
> चतुष्पदे ।  
> वि नाक॑मुख्यत्सवि॒ता वरे॒ण्योनु॑ प्र॒याण॑मु॒षसो॒ वि रा॑जति ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वा । रूपाणि । प्रति । मुंचते । कविः । प्र । असावीत् । भद्रं । द्विपदे । चतुःपदे ।  
> वि । नाकं । अख्यत् । सविता । वरेण्यः । अनु । प्रयानं । उषसः । वि । राजति ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हविर्धानप्रवर्तने रराट्यामवनद्धायां विश्वा रूपाणीत्येषानुवचनीया ।
> सूत्रितं च । विश्वा रूपाणि प्रति मुंचते कविरिति व्यवस्तायां । आ. ४-९ । इति ॥
> कविर्मेधावी सविता विश्वा सर्वाणि रूपाण्यात्मनि प्रति मुंचते । बध्नाति ।
> धारयति । किंच भद्रं कल्याणं गमनादिविषयं प्रासावीत् । अनुजानाति । कस्मै ।
> द्विपदे मनुष्याय चतुप्पदे गवाश्वादिकाय । किंच सविता सर्वस्य प्रेरको देवो
> वरेण्यो वरणीयः सन् व्यख्यत् । ख्यापयति । प्रकाशयति । किं । नाकं । नास्मिन्नकं
> दुःखमस्तीति नाकः स्वर्गः । यजमानार्थं स्वर्गं प्रकाशयतीत्यर्थः । स देव
> उषसः प्रयाणमुदयमनु वि राजति । प्रकाशते । सवितुरुदयात्पूर्वं ह्युषा
> उदेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Havirdhāna-pravartana, when the cart (रराट्यामवनद्धायां (*rarāṭyāmavanaddhāyāṃ*)) **NOTE** is bound, this Ṛk विश्वा रूपाणि (*viśvā rūpāṇi*) is the anuvacanīyā. The sūtra says: विश्वा रूपाणि प्रति मुंचते कविरिति व्यवस्तायां (*viśvā rūpāṇi prati muṃcate kaviriti vyavastāyāṃ*) (Ā. 4-9). The poet, the wise Savitṛ, binds on himself, that is, bears, all forms. Further, he has permitted what is good, auspicious, relating to going and the like. For whom? For the biped, man, and the quadruped, cattle, horses and the like. Further, Savitṛ, the god who impels all, being adorable, has made known the नाकं (*nākaṃ*); he makes it manifest. What? The नाकं (*nākaṃ*): in it there is no aka, no pain; hence it is heaven. The meaning is that he makes heaven manifest for the sacrificer. That god shines after the going forth of Uṣas, that is, her rising; for Uṣas rises before the rising of Savitṛ.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कविः (*kaviḥ*)—the wise Savitṛ
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- रूपाणि (*rūpāṇi*)—forms
- प्रति मुंचते (*prati muṃcate*)—he bears on himself
- द्विपदे (*dvipade*)—for men and others with two feet
- चतुष्पदे (*catuṣpade*)—for cattle and others with four feet
- भद्रं (*bhadraṃ*)—welfare
- प्रासावीत् (*prāsāvīt*)—he has made
- वरेण्यः (*vareṇyaḥ*)—the most venerable
- सविता (*savitā*)—Savitṛ
- नाकं (*nākaṃ*)—heaven

### Printed p. 184

- वि अख्यत् (*vi akhyat*)—he made it shine
- उषसः (*uṣasaḥ*)—of the goddess Uṣas
- प्रयाणं अनु (*prayāṇaṃ anu*)—following the path of her rising
- वि राजति (*vi rājati*)—he makes it shine

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The wise Savitṛ bears all forms on himself. He has made welfare for men and others with two feet and for cattle and others with four feet. The most venerable Savitṛ has made heaven shine. Following the path of the rising of the goddess Uṣas, he makes it shine, and shines himself.

##### English translation
The wise Savitri comprehends all forms (in himself): he has engendered what is good for biped and quadruped : the adorable Savitri has illumed the heaven, and shines in sequence to the passage of the Dawn.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

When the carts for bringing Soma, called Havirdhāna-pravartana, are brought, this Ṛk विश्वा रूपाणि (*viśvā rūpāṇi*) is to be recited as an anuvacanīyā Ṛk; this is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra **NOTE**, विश्वारूपाणि प्रति मुंचते कविरिति व्यवस्तायां (*viśvārūpāṇi prati muṃcate kaviriti vyavastāyāṃ*) (Ā. 4-9).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्य॑ प्र॒याण॒मन्व॒न्य इद्य॒युर्दे॒वा दे॒वस्य॑ महि॒मान॒मोज॑सा।  
> यः पार्थि॑वानि विम॒मे स एत॑शो॒ रजां॑सि दे॒वः स॑वि॒ता  ॒॑
> महित्वना ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यस्य । प्रयानं । अनु । अन्ये । इत् । ययुः । देवाः । देवस्य । महिमानं ।  
> ओजसा ।  
> यः । पार्थिवानि । विममे । सः । एतशः । रजांसि । देवः । सविता । महित्वना ॥ ४ ॥

**NOTE**

### Printed p. 185

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अन्य इद्देवा अन्येऽप्यग्न्यादयो यस्य देवस्य द्योतमानस्य सवितुः
> प्रयाणमनु ययुर्गच्छंति प्राप्नुवंति । किं महिमानं महत्त्वं ! सवितुरुदयाभावेऽग्निहोत्राद्यनिष्पत्तेस्तेषां हविःस्तुत्याद्यभावात् । ओजसा बलेन च युक्ता
> भवंति! यः सविता पार्थिवानि रजांसि पृथिव्यादिलोकान्महित्वना स्वमहत्त्वेन विममे
> परिच्छिन्नत्ति स देव एतश एतवर्णः शुभ्रः शोभमानः सन् । राजत इति शेषः ॥ युवशा
> ताकृणोतन । ऋग्वे. १-१६१-७ । इत्यादिवदेतश इत्यादिशकारोपजनश्चांदसः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Other gods too, Agni and the rest, follow the going forth of the shining god Savitṛ; that is, they obtain greatness. For without the rising of Savitṛ the Agnihotra and other rites are not accomplished, and those gods would lack oblation, praise and the like. They become endowed with ओजसा (*ojasā*), with strength. Savitṛ, who by his own greatness has measured out the terrestrial realms, that is, the worlds beginning with the earth, that god, एतश (*etaśa*), that is, एतवर्णः (*etavarṇaḥ*), bright, shining, shines; "he shines" is the word to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवस्य (*devasya*)—of the divine
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of which Savitṛ
- प्रयाणं अनु (*prayāṇaṃ anu*)—following his very path of going
- अन्ये इत् देवाः (*anye it devāḥ*)—all the other gods also
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—endowed with power
- महिमानं (*mahimānaṃ*)—greatness
- ययुः (*yayuḥ*)—obtain
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—the divine
- यः सविता (*yaḥ savitā*)—which Savitṛ
- महित्वना (*mahitvanā*)—by his majesty
- पार्थिवानि रजांसि (*pārthivāni rajāṃsi*)—the three worlds beginning with the earth
- विमने (*vimane*)—created and divided
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same Savitṛ
- एतशः (*etaśaḥ*)—shines, of bright colour

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Following the very path of going of the divine Savitṛ, all the other gods also obtain greatness endowed with power. That same Savitṛ, the divine one, who by his majesty created and divided the three worlds beginning with the earth, shines of bright colour.

##### English translation
After the passage of which divine (being) the other deities proceed to (obtain) majesty with power ; he who by his greatness has measured out the terrestrial regions, the divine Savitri, (is) resplendent.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त या॑सि सवित॒स्त्रीणि॑ रोच॒नोत सूर्य॑स्य र॒श्मिभिः॒ समु॑च्यसि।  
> उ॒त रात्रि॑मुभ॒यतः॒ परी॑यस उ॒त मि॒त्रो भ॑वसि देव॒  ॑
> धर्मभिः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 186

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । यासि । सवितरिति । त्रीणि । रोचना । उत । सूर्यस्य । रश्मिभिः । सं । उच्यसि ।  
> उत । रात्रीं । उभयतः । परि । ईयसे । उत । मित्रः । भवसि । देव । धर्मभिः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सवितर्देव उतापि च त्रीणि रोचना रोचमानान् द्युलोकान्यासि ।
> गच्छसि । तिस्रो दिवः पृथिवीः । ऋग्वे. ४-५३-५। इत्युक्तं । उतापि च सूर्यस्य रश्मिभिः
> समुच्यसि । संगच्छसि ॥ उच समवाय इत्यस्येदं रूपं ॥ उदयात्तूर्वभावी
> सवितोदयास्तमयवर्ति सूर्य इति । उतापि च रात्रिमुभयत उभयपार्श्वे
> परीयसे । परिगच्छसि । उतापि च हे देव सवितस्त्वं धर्मभिर्जगद्धारकैः कर्मभिर्मित्रो भवसि । मित्राख्यो देवो भवसि । अथवा प्रकाशादिप्रदानेन सखा भवसि
> सर्वजगतां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O god Savitṛ, you go to the three shining worlds, the worlds of heaven; this is said in तिस्रो दिवः पृथिवीः (*tisro divaḥ pṛthivīḥ*) (Ṛg. 4-53-5). Also you unite with the rays of Sūrya: Savitṛ is he who exists before sunrise, Sūrya is he who exists from rising to setting. Also you go around the night on both sides, on both flanks. Also, O god Savitṛ, by your acts of dharma, which uphold the world, you are Mitra, that is, you are the god called Mitra; or else, by granting light and the like, you are the friend of all the world.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—moreover
- सवितः (*savitaḥ*)—O god Savitṛ
- त्रीणि रोचना (*trīṇi rocanā*)—the three shining worlds
- यासि (*yāsi*)—you travel
- उत (*uta*)—and
- सूरस्य (*sūrasya*)—of Sūra
- रश्मिभिः (*raśmibhiḥ*)—with the rays
- समुच्यसि (*samucyasi*)—you unite
- उत (*uta*)—and
- रात्रीं (*rātrīṃ*)—the night
- उभयतः (*ubhayataḥ*)—on both sides
- परि ईयसे (*pari īyase*)—you go around
- उत (*uta*)—more than all this
- देव (*deva*)—O divine Savitṛ
- धर्मभिः (*dharmabhiḥ*)—by acts that uphold the world
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—in the form of Mitra
- भवसि (*bhavasi*)—you become

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O god Savitṛ, you travel through the three shining worlds. You become one with the rays of the sun. You go around the night, encircling it on both sides. More than all this, by your acts that uphold the world you become Mitra in form.

##### English translation
Either you traverse, Savitri, the three regions, or combine with the rays of Surya ; or you pass, between the night on either hand ; or you divine Savitri, are Mitra, through your (benevolent) functions.

### Printed p. 187

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

त्रीणि रोचनोत सूर्यस्य (*trīṇi rocanota sūryasya*)—The commentator has cited a Ṛk saying that the sun holds the three kinds of atmospheric worlds under his control:

त्रिरंतरिक्षं सविता महित्वना त्री रजांसि परिभूस्त्रीणि रोचना । तिस्रो दिवः पृथिवीस्तिस्त्र इन्वति त्रिभिव्रतैरभि नो रक्षति त्मना ॥ (*triraṃtarikṣaṃ savitā mahitvanā trī rajāṃsi paribhūstrīṇi rocanā | tisro divaḥ pṛthivīstistra invati tribhivratairabhi no rakṣati tmanā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-53-5)

The idea suggested here is that Savitṛ holds under his control the three kinds of atmospheric worlds and the three kinds of earthly worlds, and protects them by his own impulse. These are: the worlds Vāyu, Vidyut, Varuṇa, or Agni, Vāyu, Āditya, which are the intermediate divisions of the atmospheric world. In the same way the three worlds Indra, Prajāpati and Satya are the intermediate divisions of the heavenly world; and likewise the earth (Bhū) world has three intermediate divisions, as the commentator has explained.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒तेशि॑षे प्रस॒वस्य॒ त्वमेक॒ इदु॒त पू॒षा भ॑वसि देव॒ याम॑भिः ।  
> उ॒तेदं विश्वं॒ भुव॑नं॒ वि रा॑जसि श्या॒वाश्व॑स्ते सवितः॒ स्तोम॑मानशे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । ईशिशे । प्रसवस्य । त्वं । एकः । इत् । उत । पूषा । भवसि । देव । यामभिः ।  
> उत । इदं । विश्वं । भुवनं । वि । राजसि । श्यावअश्वः । ते । सवितरिति । स्तोमं ।  
> आनशे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सवितस्त्वमेक इत् एक एव प्रसवस्य सर्वकर्मानुज्ञाकरणस्येशिषे ।
> समर्थो भवसि । ये लौकिकं गमनादिरूपं वैदिकमग्निहोत्रादिरूपं कर्मानुतिष्ठंते तेषां सर्वेषामनुज्ञातुमेक एव प्रभवसि । उतापि च पूषा पोषको
> भवसि हे देव यामभिर्गमनैः । उतापि चेदं विश्वं भुवनं भूतजातं वि राजसि ।

### Printed p. 188

> ईशिषे धारयितुं । यस्मादेवं महानुभावस्तस्मात् हे सवितस्ते शुभ्यं श्यावाश्व
> ऋषिः स्तोमं स्तोत्रमानशे । व्याप्न्नोति । करोतीत्येवमात्मानं परोक्षतया
> निर्दिशन्नाह ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Savitṛ, you alone have power over प्रसवस्य (*prasavasya*), the giving of leave for all rites. Of all who perform rites, worldly ones such as going and Vedic ones such as the Agnihotra, you alone are able to give leave. Also you are Pūṣan, the nourisher, O god, by your goings. Also you shine over this whole world, all that has come to be; you are able to uphold it. Since you are of such great power, O Savitṛ, the seer Śyāvāśva has made this praise for you; thus he speaks of himself in the third person.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you (O Savitṛ)
- एक इत् (*eka it*)—alone
- प्रसवस्य (*prasavasya*)—of all the beings that have come into being
- ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*)—you are the lord
- उत (*uta*)—and
- देव (*deva*)—O Savitṛ
- यामभि (*yāmabhi*)—by your acts of going
- पूषा (*pūṣā*)—in the form of Pūṣan
- भवसि (*bhavasi*)—you are
- उत (*uta*)—moreover
- इदं विश्वं भुवनं (*idaṃ viśvaṃ bhuvanaṃ*)—this whole world
- वि राजसि (*vi rājasi*)—you rule
- सवितः (*savitaḥ*)—O god Savitṛ
- श्यावाश्वः (*śyāvāśvaḥ*)—the seer Śyāvāśva
- ते (*te*)—addressing you
- स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)—this praise
- आनशे (*ānaśe*)—offers

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Savitṛ, you alone are the lord of all beings that have come into being; by your acts of going you yourself are called the god Pūṣan. You are the sovereign of the whole world and rule over all. O god Savitṛ, the seer Śyāvāśva offers this praise addressed to you.

##### English translation
You alone rule over (the actions of) living beings : you are Pushan divine (Savitri), by your movements; you are sovereign over the whole world : Shyavashwa offers praise, Savitri, to you.

## Sūkta 82 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तत्सवितुरिति नवर्चं दशमं सूक्तं श्यावाश्वस्यार्षं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका ।
> तत्सवितुर्नव गायत्रमाद्यानुष्टुबिति । प्रथमानुष्टुप् शिष्टा गायत्रः । सावित्रं
> त्वित्युक्तत्वात्सावित्रं ॥ अग्निष्टोमे वैश्वदेवशस्त्रस्याद्यस्तृचः प्रतिपत् आद्या नो
> देवेत्यनुचरः । सूत्रितं च । तत्सवितुर्वृणीमहेऽद्या नो देव सवितरिति वैश्वदेवस्य
> प्रतिपदनुचरा । आ. ५. १८. । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is the tenth sūkta, of nine Ṛks, the vision of Śyāvāśva. On it the Anukramaṇikā says: तत्सवितुर्नव गायत्रमाद्यानुष्टुबिति (*tatsaviturnava gāyatramādyānuṣṭubiti*). The first Ṛk is Anuṣṭubh, the rest are Gāyatra. Because the Anukramaṇikā calls it Sāvitra, the deity is Savitṛ. In the Agniṣṭoma, the first tṛca of the Vaiśvadeva-śastra is the pratipad and the three beginning अद्या नो देव (*adyā no deva*) are the anucara. The sūtra says: तत्सवितुर्वृणीमहेऽद्या नो देव सवितरिति वैश्वदेवस्य प्रतिपदनुचरा (*tatsaviturvṛṇīmahe 'dyā no deva savitariti vaiśvadevasya pratipadanucarā*) (Ā. 5. 18.).

### Printed p. 189

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning तत्सवितुः (*tatsavituḥ*) is the tenth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: तत्सवितुर्नव गायत्रमाद्यानुष्टुप् (*tatsaviturnava gāyatramādyānuṣṭup*). The seer of this sūkta is Śyāvāśva Ātreya, and the deity is Savitṛ. The first Ṛk of this sūkta is in the Anuṣṭubh metre, the remaining Ṛks in the Gāyatrī metre. In the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice, when the Vaiśvadeva-śastra mantras are recited, the first three Ṛks of this sūkta are to be recited as the pratipad tṛca, and the three Ṛks beginning अद्या नो देव (*adyā no deva*) (Ṛks 4-6 of this sūkta) as the anucara tṛca; this is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, तत्सवितुर्वृणीमहेऽद्या नो देव सवितरिति वैश्वेदेवस्य प्रतिपदनुचर् (*tatsaviturvṛṇīmahe 'dyā no deva savitariti vaiśvedevasya pratipadanucar*) (Ā. 5-18).

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 82
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 25, 26
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9
- Seer: Śyāvāśva Ātreya
- Deity: Savitṛ
- Metre: Ṛk 1 Anuṣṭubh; Ṛks 2-9 Gāyatrī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तत्स॑वि॒तुर्वृ॑णीमहे व॒यं दे॒वस्य॒ भोज॑नं ।  
> श्रेष्ठं॑ सर्व॒धात॑मं॒ तुरं॒ भग॑स्य धीमहि ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । सवितुः । वृणेमहे । वयं । देवस्य । भोजनं ।  
> श्रेष्ठं । सर्वधातमं । तुरं । भगस्य । धीमहि ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तत् प्राप्यत्वेन प्रसिद्धं भोजनं भोग्यं धनं वयं स्तोतारो वृणीमहे
> प्रार्थयामः । कस्य । धनं । सवितुः प्रेरकस्य देवस्य स्वभूतं । लब्ध्वा च श्रेष्ठं
> प्रशस्यं सर्वधातमं सर्वधातृतमं । सर्वभोगप्रदमित्यर्थः । तुरं शत्रूणां
> हिंसकं । धनेन शत्रून्हंतुं शक्यत्वात् । तादृशं धनं भगस्य भजनीयस्य सवितुरनु

### Printed p. 190

> ग्रहाद्दीमहि । धारायाम । उपभोगं करवामेत्यर्थः । अथवा धनं वृणे
> महेऽर्थित्वाच्च लभेमहीति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

We, the singers, ask for that enjoyable wealth, well known as what is to be obtained. Whose? The wealth that belongs to the impelling god Savitṛ. Having obtained it, it is excellent, praiseworthy, सर्वधातमं (*sarvadhātamaṃ*), that is, giver of all enjoyments; तुरं (*turaṃ*), destroying foes, for with wealth enemies can be slain. Such wealth, by the favour of Bhaga, the adorable Savitṛ, we would hold, that is, enjoy. Or else: we ask for wealth, and, because of our desire, may we obtain it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवस्य (*devasya*)—of the divine
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—Savitṛ
- तत् (*tat*)—well known
- भोजनं (*bhojanaṃ*)—and fit for enjoyment, the wealth (so as to obtain it)
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- वृणीमहे (*vṛṇīmahe*)—desire and pray for
- भगस्य (*bhagasya*)—of the venerable Bhaga (Savitṛ)
- श्रेष्ठं (*śreṣṭhaṃ*)—the most excellent
- सर्वधातुवं (*sarvadhātuvaṃ*)—exceedingly fit for enjoyment
- तुरं (*turaṃ*)—and destroying foes, the wealth
- धीमहि (*dhīmahi*)—may we obtain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

We desire and pray to obtain the well-known wealth of the divine Savitṛ, fit for enjoyment. May we obtain the wealth of the venerable Bhaga, most excellent, exceedingly fit for enjoyment and destroying foes.

##### English translation
We solicit of the divine Savitri enjoyable (wealth) ; may we receive from Bhaga that which is excellent, all-sustaining, destructive of foes.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अस्य॒ हि स्वय॑शस्तरं सवि॒तः कच्च॒न प्रि॒यं ।  
> न मि॒नंति॑ स्व॒राज्यं॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्य । हि । स्वयशःतरं । सवितुः । कत् । चन । प्रियं ।  
> न । मिनंति । स्वराज्यं ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 191

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्य सवितुः स्वयशस्तरं स्वयमसाधारणं यशो यस्यातिशयेन भवति
> तत्तादृशं प्रियं सर्वेषां प्रियभूतं स्वराज्यं स्वयमेव राजमानत्वमैश्वर्य्यं
> कच्चन केचिदप्यसुरादयो न मिनंति । न हिंसंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of this Savitṛ, whose fame is exceedingly his own and uncommon, such a dear sovereignty, स्वराज्यं (*svarājyaṃ*), that is, shining by himself, lordship, dear to all, no one, no asuras and the like, impairs; they do not injure it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आस्य (*āsya*)—of the well-known
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—god Savitṛ
- स्वयशस्तरं (*svayaśastaraṃ*)—that which is itself the cause of his uncommon fame
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—and dear to the whole world
- स्वराज्यं (*svarājyaṃ*)—sovereignty
- कच्चन (*kaccana*)—no adversary even
- न मिनंति (*na minaṃti*)—can injure

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

No adversary can injure the sovereignty and the wealth of Savitṛ, which is the cause of his uncommon fame by his own greatness and is dear to the whole world.

##### English translation
Nothing impairs the sovereignty of this Savitri, which is most especially renowned and beloved.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स हि रत्ना॑नि दा॒शुषे॑ सु॒वाति॑ सवि॒ता भगः॑ ।  
> तं भा॒गं चि॒त्रमी॑महे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । हि । रत्नानि । दाशुषे । सुवाति । सविता । भगः ।  
> तं । भागं । चित्रं । ईमहे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स हि स खलु सविता भगो भजनीयो देवो दाशुषे हविर्दात्रे मह्यं
> रत्नानि रमणीयानि धनानि सुवाति । प्रेरयति । प्रयच्छति । तं देवं भागं भजनीयं
> चित्रं चायनीयं धनमीमहे । याचामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

He, indeed, Savitṛ Bhaga, the adorable god, gives, that is, sends and bestows, jewels, delightful riches, on me, the giver of oblation. That god's share, the adorable one, the wonderful wealth, चायनीयं (*cāyanīyaṃ*), to be gathered, we ask for; we beg.

### Printed p. 192

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भगः (*bhagaḥ*)—the adorable one
- स हि सविता (*sa hi savitā*)—that same god Savitṛ
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—to the sacrificer, the giver of oblation
- रत्नानि (*ratnāni*)—delightful riches
- सुवाति (*suvāti*)—gives
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—attractive
- तं भागं (*taṃ bhāgaṃ*)—a share of that wealth (that praiseworthy wealth)
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we desire and pray for

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The adorable Savitṛ gives delightful riches to the sacrificer who is the giver of oblation. We desire and pray for that wealth, which is attractive and worthy of worship.

##### English translation
That savitri, who is Bhaga,bestows precious treasure on the donor
of the offering: we solicit (of him) a valuable portion.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒द्या नो॑ देव सवितः प्र॒जाव॑त्सावीः॒ सौभ॑गं ।  
> परा दुःष्वप्नं सुव ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अद्य । नः । देव । सवितरिति । प्रजावत् । सावीः । सौभगं ।  
> परा । दुःस्वप्नं । सुव ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अद्या न इति चतुर्थीपंचमीभ्याममनोज्ञस्वप्नदर्शने प्रत्यृचं जुहुयात् । अद्या नो देव सवितरिति द्वाभ्यां । आ, गृ. ३-६-६ । इति सूत्रितं ॥
> हे सवितर्देव नोऽस्मभ्यमध्यासि न्यागदिने प्रजावत्पुत्राद्युपेतं सौभगं
> धनं सावीः । प्रेरय । दुष्स्वप्नं दुःस्वप्नं दुःस्वप्नवद्दुःखकरं दारिद्रं परासुव
> दूरे प्रेरय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa states the ritual use: when an unpleasant dream has been seen, one should offer oblation with each Ṛk, with the fourth and the fifth Ṛk, as the Gṛhya-sūtra ordains: अद्या नो देव सवितरिति द्वाभ्यां (*adyā no deva savitariti dvābhyāṃ*) (Ā. Gṛ. 3-6-6). He then explains the verse. O god Savitṛ, to us, today, in this sacrificial day, send wealth, सौभगं (*saubhagaṃ*), that is endowed with offspring, sons and the like. Send far away the bad dream, दुःस्वप्नं (*duḥsvapnaṃ*), that is, poverty, which is painful like a bad dream. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 193

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—adorable
- सवितः (*savitaḥ*)—O god Savitṛ
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this sacrificial day
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- प्रजावत् (*prajāvat*)—endowed with sons and the like
- सौभगं (*saubhagaṃ*)—wealth
- सावीः (*sāvīḥ*)—provide
- दुःस्वप्नं (*duḥsvapnaṃ*)—the troubles such as poverty, painful like a bad dream
- परा सुव (*parā suva*)—drive far away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O adorable god Savitṛ, provide us in this sacrifice with wealth endowed with sons and the like. Drive far away the troubles such as poverty, which are painful like a bad dream.

##### English translation
Grant us to-day, divine Savitri, affluence with progeny, and drive away evil dreams.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

When a bad dream (a nightmare) has occurred, for the removal of its evil one should offer oblation of ghee in the fire with this Ṛk beginning अद्या नः (*adyā naḥ*) and with the next Ṛk beginning विश्वानि देव (*viśvāni deva*). This is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Gṛhya-sūtra, अद्या नो देव सवितरिति द्वाभ्यां (*adyā no deva savitariti dvābhyāṃ*) (Ā. Gṛ. 3-6-6).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्वा॑नि देव सवितर्दुरि॒तानि॒ परा॑ सुव ।  
> यद्भ॒द्रं तन्न॒ आ सु॑व ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वानि । देव । सवितः । दुःइतानि । परा । सुव ।  
> यत् । भद्रं । तत् । नः । आ । सुव ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सवितर्देव त्वं विश्वानि दुरितानि परा सुव । यद्भद्रं प्रजापशुगृहादिकं
> तन्नोऽस्मभ्यमा सुव । अस्मदभिमुखं प्रेरय । प्रजा पै भद्रं पशवो भद्रं गृहं
> भद्रमिति हि श्रुतिः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O god Savitṛ, drive away all evils, दुरितानि (*duritāni*). Whatever is auspicious, भद्रं (*bhadraṃ*), such as offspring, cattle and house, that bring to us, आ सुव (*ā suva*), that is, send it towards us. For the scripture says that offspring is auspicious, cattle are auspicious, the house is auspicious: प्रजा पै भद्रं पशवो भद्रं गृहं भद्रमिति हि श्रुतिः ॥ (*prajā pai bhadraṃ paśavo bhadraṃ gṛhaṃ bhadramiti hi śrutiḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 194

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—adorable
- सवितः (*savitaḥ*)—O Savitṛ
- विश्वानि (*viśvāni*)—all
- दुरितानि (*duritāni*)—sins (misfortunes)
- परा सुव (*parā suva*)—drive far away and destroy
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever
- भद्रं (*bhadraṃ*)—is auspicious
- तत् (*tat*)—that
- आ सुव (*ā suva*)—provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O adorable Savitṛ, drive far away and destroy all our sins and misfortunes. Provide us with whatever is auspicious for us.

##### English translation
Remove from us, divine Savitri, all misfortunes : bestow upon us
that which is good.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अना॑गसो॒ अदि॑तये दे॒वस्य॑ सवि॒तुः स॒वे ।  
> विश्वा॑ ना॒मानि॑ धीमहि ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अनागसः । अदितये । देवस्य । सवितुः । सवे '  
> विश्वा । नामानि । धीमहि ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयमनुष्ठातारः सवितुः प्रेरकस्य देवस्य सवेऽनुज्ञायां सत्यामदितयेऽखंडनीयायै देव्यै भूम्या अनागसः स्याम । अनपराधिनो भवेम ।
> भूम्यां हि पापाः संभवंति । असगतेष्वागःसु विश्वा सर्वाणि वानीयानि
> धनानि धीमहि । धारयाम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

We, the performers, when Savitṛ the impelling god gives his sanction, may be free from offence, अनागसः (*anāgasaḥ*), that is, without fault, towards Aditi, the indivisible goddess, who is the Earth; for it is on the earth that sins arise. **NOTE** We hold, धीमहि (*dhīmahi*), that is, we bear, all desirable riches.

### Printed p. 195

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवस्य (*devasya*)—of the divine
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—of Savitṛ
- सवे (*save*)—obedient to the command
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- आदितये (*āditaye*)—in regard to the goddess Aditi
- अनागसः (*anāgasaḥ*)—let us be free from sin
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- वामानि (*vāmāni*)—also the desired riches
- धीमहि (*dhīmahi*)—let us obtain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Being obedient to the command of the divine Savitṛ, may we be free from sin in regard to the goddess Aditi. May we obtain all the desired riches.

##### English translation
Let us be void of offence towards Aditi, according to the will of the divine Savitri: may we be possessed of all-desired (riches).

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ वि॒श्वदे॑वं॒ सत्प॑तिं सू॒क्तैर॒द्या वृ॑णीमहे ।  
> सत्यसनं सवितारं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । विश्वदेवं । सर्पपतिं । सुउक्तैः । अद्य । वृणेमहे ।  
> सत्यसवं । सवितारं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> चातुर्मास्येषु वैश्वदेवे पर्वणि सावित्रस्य द्वादशकपालस्या विश्वदेवमित्यनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं वाममद्य सवितः । आ. २-१६ । इति ॥
> प्रायणीय एषैव सवितुरनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं य इमा विश्वा
> जातानि । आ. ४ ३ । इति ॥ देवसुवां हविष्वेष्टैव सवितुरनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आ
> विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं न प्रमिये सवितुर्द्वैव्यस्य तत् । आ. ४-११ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa gives the ritual use in three places. In the Cāturmāsya sacrifice, at the Vaiśvadeva parvan, this Ṛk, beginning आ विश्वदेवं (*ā viśvadevaṃ*), is the anuvākyā for the oblation to Savitṛ on twelve potsherds; the sūtra says so (Ā. 2-16). In the Prāyaṇīya offering this same Ṛk is the anuvākyā to Savitṛ; the sūtra says so (Ā. 4 3). **NOTE** In the offering of the oblations called Devasū this same Ṛk is again the anuvākyā to Savitṛ; the sūtra says so (Ā. 4-11).

### Printed p. 196

> विश्वदेवं । विश्वे देवा यस्य वशे भवंति तं तादृशं । तं हि सर्वात्मत्वादिंद्रं
> मित्रं वरुणमग्निमाहुरित्यादिश्रुतेरितरेषां तद्विभूतित्वात् । सत्पतिं सतामनुष्ठात्याणां पालकं सत्यसवं सत्यानुज्ञं सवितारं देवमद्यास्मिन्यागदिने सूक्तैरावृणीमहे । संभजामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

विश्वदेवं (*viśvadevaṃ*): he under whose control all the gods stand, such a one. He is all-pervading and the self of all; for the scripture says that they call him Indra, Mitra, Varuṇa, Agni, and so on, the others being his manifestations. सत्पतिं (*satpatiṃ*): the protector of the good, of those who perform the rites. सत्यसवं (*satyasavaṃ*): whose sanction is true. We today, on this sacrificial day, choose and serve the god Savitṛ with hymns, सूक्तैः (*sūktaiḥ*). **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विश्वदेवं (*viśvadevaṃ*)—who is the inner controller in all the gods
- सत्पतिं (*satpatiṃ*)—the protector of the good
- सत्यसवं (*satyasavaṃ*)—who has a regulation of the nature of truth
- सवितारं (*savitāraṃ*)—the god Savitṛ
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this day of the sacrifice
- सूक्तैः (*sūktaiḥ*)—with excellent praises
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we praise and worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I praise and worship the god Savitṛ, who is the inner controller in all the gods, the protector of the good, and who has a regulation of the nature of truth, on this day of the sacrifice, with excellent praises.

##### English translation
We glorify to-day with hymns Savitri, the protector of the good,
the observer of truth, (identical with) all the gods.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the sacrifice called Cāturmāsya, in the Vaiśvadeva parvan, in the oblation on twelve potsherds made with Savitṛ as the deity, this Ṛk beginning आ विश्वदेवं (*ā viśvadevaṃ*) is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं वाममद्य सवितः (*ā viśvadevaṃ satpatiṃ vāmamadya savitaḥ*) (Ā. 2-16). In the Prāyaṇīya offering also, in the oblation made with Savitṛ as the deity, this same Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं य इमा विश्वा जातानि (*ā viśvadevaṃ satpatiṃ ya imā viśvā jātāni*) (Ā. 4-3). And in the oblations called Devasū, in the oblation made to Savitṛ as the deity, this Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं न प्रमिये सवितुर्दैव्यस्य तत् (*ā viśvadevaṃ satpatiṃ na pramiye saviturdaivyasya tat*) (Ā. 4-11).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य इ॒मे उ॒भे अह॑नी पु॒र एत्यप्र॑युच्छन् ।  
> स्वा॒धीर्दे॒वः स॑वि॒ता ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 197

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । इमे इति । उभे इति । अहनी इति । पुरः । एति । अप्रयुच्छन् ।  
> सुआधीः । देवः । सविता ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यः सविता देवः स्वाधीः शोभनाध्यानः सुकर्मा वा सन् इमे अहनी
> रात्र्यहनी तयोः पुरः पुरस्तावप्रयुच्छन् अप्रमाद्यन्नेति गच्छति तमा वृणीमह
> इति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The god Savitṛ, who, being स्वाधीः (*svādhīḥ*), of good meditation or of good works, goes in front of these two, day and night, अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*), that is, without negligence: him we choose. Thus the construction is completed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Savitṛ who
- इमे उभे अहनी (*ime ubhe ahanī*)—of both these day and night
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—in front (as a guide)
- अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*)—very carefully
- एति (*eti*)—moves about (such)
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—divine
- सविता (*savitā*)—Savitṛ
- स्वाधीः (*svādhīḥ*)—is the object of excellent meditation

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Savitṛ who moves about as a guide in front of both day and night, free from negligence and very careful, that same god is the object of excellent meditation.

##### English translation
The divine object of meditation, Savitri, who, ever vigilant, precedes
both night and day.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य इ॒मा विश्वा॑ जा॒तान्या॑श्रा॒वय॑ति॒ श्लोके॑न ।  
> प्र च॑ सु॒वाति॑ सवि॒ता ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यॆः । इमा । विश्वा । जातानि । आश्रवयति । श्लोकेन ।  
> प्र । च । सुवाति । सविता ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 198

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रायणीये य इमा विश्वेति सवितुर्याज्या । सूत्रितं च । य इमा विश्वा
> जातानि सुत्रमाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं । आ. ४-३ । इति ॥ अश्वमेधेश्वमुत्सृज्यानु
> सवनं सावित्रीष्टिः कार्या । तस्यामेषा माध्यंदिने सवनेऽनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च ।
> य इमा विश्वा जातान्या देवो यातु सविता सुरत्नः । आ. १०-६ । इति ॥
> यो देव इमेमानि विश्वा सर्वाणि जातान्युत्पन्नान् प्राणिनः । जंगमानित्यर्थः । तान् श्लोकेन यशसाश्रावयति । सर्वेऽप्यस्य स्तुतिं शृण्वंतीत्यर्थः । अथवा
> गर्जनशब्देन सर्वाणीमान्युत्पन्नान्याश्रावयति वृष्ट्युन्मुखः सन् । किंच सविता प्र
> सुवाति प्रेरयति तं वृणीमह इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa gives three ritual uses. In the Prāyaṇīya offering the Ṛk beginning य इमा विश्वा (*ya imā viśvā*) is the yājyā for Savitṛ; the sūtra says: य इमा विश्वा जातानि सुत्रमाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं (*ya imā viśvā jātāni sutramāṇaṃ pṛthivīṃ dyāmanehasaṃ*) (Ā. 4-3). In the Aśvamedha, after the horse has been let loose, a Sāvitrī offering is to be performed at each pressing; in it this Ṛk is the anuvākyā at the midday pressing; the sūtra says: य इमा विश्वा जातान्या देवो यातु सविता सुरत्नः (*ya imā viśvā jātānyā devo yātu savitā suratnaḥ*) (Ā. 10-6). Then the sense of the verse: the god who makes all these born beings, that is, creatures, meaning moving ones, hear श्लोकेन (*ślokena*), that is, by fame; the meaning is that all hear his praise. Or else, eager to give rain, he makes all these born beings hear by the sound of his thunder. And further, Savitṛ impels, sends forth; him we choose.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which
- सविता (*savitā*)—god Savitṛ
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- इमा जातानि (*imā jātāni*)—these living beings
- श्लोकेन (*ślokena*)—by his own greatness
- अश्रावयति (*aśrāvayati*)—makes manifest
- च (*ca*)—and
- प्र सुवाति (*pra suvāti*)—gives life to all living beings (such a god Savitṛ we worship)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The god Savitṛ who by his own greatness makes all living beings manifest, and who gives life to all living beings, such a god Savitṛ we worship.

##### English translation
Savitri, who proclaims his glory to all these living beings. and gives them life.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Prāyaṇīya offering, this Ṛk beginning य इमा विश्वा (*ya imā viśvā*) is to be recited as the yājyā mantra in the oblation made with Savitṛ as the deity; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra य इमा विश्वा जातानि सुत्रामाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं (*ya imā viśvā jātāni sutrāmāṇaṃ pṛthivīṃ dyāmanehasaṃ*) (Ā. 4-6). **NOTE** And in the Aśvamedha sacrifice, after the horse has been let loose to wander, the after-pressing offering called सावित्रिपति (*sāvitripati*) is to be performed. In that offering, at the time of the midday pressing, this Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra य इमा विश्वा जातान्या देवो यातु सविता सुरत्नः (*ya imā viśvā jātānyā devo yātu savitā suratnaḥ*) (Ā. 10-6). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 199

## Sūkta 83 (Maṇḍala 5)

**Introduction**

**A brief account of the deity Parjanya**

Although the subject of the deity Parjanya is indicated in many Ṛks of the Ṛgveda, the sūktas that praise this deity alone are only three. Among them the 83rd sūkta, which is the present subject, is very important. There are ten Ṛks in all in it. In these Ṛks the nature of the deity Parjanya is well described. On the question who this deity Parjanya is, Yāska has given the etymology: पर्जन्यः । पर्जनस्यपेराद्यंतविषरीतस्य तर्पयिता जन्यः । परो जेता ना । परो जनयिता वा । प्रार्जयिता वा रसानां ॥ (*parjanyaḥ | parjanasyaperādyaṃtaviṣarītasya tarpayitā janyaḥ | paro jetā nā | paro janayitā vā | prārjayitā vā rasānāṃ ‖*) (Ni. 10-10). That is, the word parjanya is formed from the root तृप (*tṛpa*) (to satisfy). By transposing the letters of this root and by adding the (qualifying) word जन्य (*janya*), it becomes पर्जन्य (*parjanya*). How: in तृप (*tṛpa*), if the ṛ-letter (र् (*r*)) of the first syllable is added at the end, and the last letter, प (*pa*), at the beginning, there results पर् (*par*). If जन्य (*janya*) is added to this, पर्जन्य (*parjanya*) results. Because he pours down the rain (vṛṣṭi), which is most necessary to people and productive of food and the like, he satisfies the people: this is the component meaning of the word Parjanya. Or परो जेता वा (*paro jetā vā*): it may also mean one who is excellent and victorious. Or परो जनयिता वा (*paro janayitā vā*): or it may mean that this god, by pouring rain, is the producer of plants and the like and of food and the like. Or प्रार्थयिता वा रसानां (*prārthayitā vā rasānāṃ*): rasa means water; since he provides water to the people by means of rain, this god is named Parjanya. In this way Yāska has explained the formation of the word Parjanya. To explain this meaning he has given the second Ṛk of this very sūkta as an example and has also stated its etymology; we have explained it in the particulars of that Ṛk. The main purport is that in this sūkta the god who is the cause of rain, accompanied by thunder, lightning, flashes and storm, is praised as Parjanya.

This Parjanya is praised not only in this sūkta but also in the 101st and 102nd sūktas of the seventh Maṇḍala; in the 101st there are only six Ṛks and in the 102nd only three Ṛks. But the nature, qualities and so on of Parjanya are not set forth so clearly as in the present sūkta. In the seventh

### Printed p. 200

Maṇḍala, although the deity of the 12th sūkta is the Maṇḍūkas (frogs), by option (in meaning) that sūkta also is concerned with Parjanya.

Parjanya is the lord of all moving beings. (यो विश्वस्य जगतो देव ईशे (*yo viśvasya jagato deva īśe*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-2); in this god all the worlds, and the three worlds of the atmosphere, are established. That is, this god is the support of all the worlds. (यस्मिन्विश्वा भुवनानि तस्थुस्तिस्रो द्यावस्त्रेधा सस्तुरापः (*yasminviśvā bhuvanāni tasthustisro dyāvastredhā sasturāpaḥ*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-4); in this god the self of all beings abides. That is, this god is the impeller of all beings, moving and unmoving. (तस्मिन्नात्मा जगतस्तस्थुषश्च (*tasminnātmā jagatastasthuṣaśca*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-6); this god is said to be the son of heaven or of the heaven-deity (दिवस्पुत्राय (*divasputrāya*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-102-1). The subject of the deity Parjanya is stated not only in the sūktas mentioned above but also in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-57-8; 5-63-4, 6; 6-52-6; 7-55-10; 8-6-1; 8-21-18; 9-2-9; 9-22-2; 10-66-6; 10-98-1, 8; 10-169-2. And in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-49-6; 6-50-12; 10-65-9; 10-66-10 he is praised together with the deity Vāta (Vāyu), and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-52-16 together with Agni, as a dual deity: पर्जन्यावाता (*parjanyāvātā*), वातापर्जन्या (*vātāparjanyā*), अग्नीपर्जन्यौ (*agnīparjanyau*). And in Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-82-3 there is the description that Parjanya is the father of the Soma plant (पर्जन्य पिता महिषस्य पर्णिनः (*parjanya pitā mahiṣasya parṇinaḥ*)).

The place of this deity in the Ṛgveda is very unimportant: this deity is praised in only three sūktas, and this name occurs only thirty times. In the Atharvaveda also (4-15) one sūkta has Parjanya as its deity, but the Ṛks in that sūkta are for the most part chosen from the Ṛgveda. In the passages that follow, Parjanya generally means simply the familiar cloud. The rain-clouds (Parjanya) quicken the earth (1-164-51). The Maruts make darkness even by day with the water-bearing clouds (Parjanya) (1-38-9). They spread the clouds in both worlds (5-53-6). Brhaspati is prayed to make the rain fall and to send the rain-cloud (10-98-1 and 8). Soma flows like a rain-cloud (9-2-9), and the Soma drops fall as rain falls from a cloud (9-22-2). In the Atharvaveda, in praising the rain-pouring cow called Vaśā, it is said: "O Parjanya, your udder" (A. Ne. 10-10-7). But in (Vā. Saṃ. 12-6) Parjanya means Stanayitnu (lightning, thunder), and in (Śa. Brā. 14-5-5-10) it is used in the sense of Dyauḥ (Vā. Saṃ. 12-6). **NOTE** In some contexts it is difficult to say whether the conventional meaning or the deity is intended. Like Parjanya, Agni's power gives an echo (8-91-5). Awakened by Parjanya the frogs croak (7-103-1). That Parjanya means the deity is clearly seen in many passages.

### Printed p. 201

In such contexts the cloud is called by names such as udder, pot, water-bag (5-83-8 and 9; 7-101-4). To some extent the deity Parjanya is described as an animal such as a bull. The bull, full of swiftly flowing water and bellowing loudly (compare 5-83-1; 5-83-6 and 9; A. Ve. 4-15-1); the water that comes from the hissing, watery bull and roars gladdens the earth (A. Ve. 4-15-1). There are descriptions that Parjanya at times is a barren cow, at times a milch cow, and that Parjanya uses his body as he wishes (7-101-3). Pouring rain is the main work of Parjanya. Moving about in a chariot made of water (5-83-7), he unties the water-bag and holds it mouth downwards. As a charioteer urges on his horses, Parjanya shows forth his rain-messengers; when the rain falls, a lion's roar is heard from afar. Pouring rain, the roaring Parjanya comes like our heavenly father (Asura) (5-83-3 and 6). He is prayed to pour rain (7-101-5) and, when he has poured, to stop (5-83-10). Parjanya and the Maruts pour rain. While remaining subject to Mitra and Varuṇa (5-63-3 to 6), it is also said that he often roars (5-83). Roaring, he overthrows trees, demons, evil-doers, all. The whole world is afraid of his weapon (5-83-2). He and Vāta are both the ones who wield that weapon (10-66-10). There is also some connection with the thunderbolt. When Parjanya quickens the earth with seeds, the wind blows and lightning strikes (5-83-4). Parjanya, together with the lightning, thunders in the ocean (A. Ve. 19-30-4).

Because he pours rain, Parjanya is the cause of the growth of plants. When seed is sown in the earth, the plants spring up. All kinds of plants come within his sphere of action; for nourishment he has produced the plants (5-83-4, 5 and 10; compare 6-52-6; A. Ve. 4-15-2, 3 and 15; 8-7-21). He is the one who makes plants increase and bear fruit. Protected by that deity, the plants bear good fruit (7-101-1 and 5). By his action reeds and grass grow (7-102-1; compare 5-75-15; A. Ve. 1-2-1; 1-3-1; 9-30-5). Parjanya places the seed (the embryonic germ) not only in plants but also in cows, mares and women (7-102-2), and he is prayed to for fertility (5-83-7; compare 6-52-16). He is the bull that makes everything fruitful; in him is the self of all things moving and unmoving in the world (7-101-6; compare 1-15-1).

### Printed p. 202

He is the ruler of the whole world, a king of complete independence; in him all beings and the three worlds are established. In him the threefold water flows (7-101-2, 4 and 5). Because of this generative power the epithet "father" is applied to him many times (7-101-3; 9-82-3; A. Ve. 4-15-12; 12-1-12). He is our (king, father) heavenly father (5-83-6).

The earth is suggested to be his wife (5-83-4; 7-101-3; compare 1-160-3). In the Atharvaveda (12-1-12) the earth is the mother and Parjanya the father, and in another place (11-10-6) it is stated clearly that one called Vaśā is his wife. In calling himself a bull and in being connected with thunder, lightning and rain, Parjanya is equal to the heaven-deity (compare 10-45 4; 2-4-6; 2-27-15); in one place it is said that Parjanya is the son of the heaven-deity (7-102-1). Parjanya himself produces a calf; this is the sprout of the plants (7-101-1; compare 5-83-1). This calf may be the lightning or Soma. In one place Parjanya is said to be the father of Soma (9-82-3), and that it grew through Parjanya (9-113-3).

Parjanya has connection with many other deities, but the connection is greatest with Vāta. Except for one occasion with Agni, it is with Parjanya alone that Vāta is spoken of as a dual deity. The Maruts also are praised together with Parjanya (5-63-6; 5-83-5); they praise Parjanya (A. Ve. 4-15-4). In two mantras Agni is praised together with him (6-52-6 and 16). Parjanya, who gives rain, and Indra have many qualities in common (8-6-1); the same part of nature is the basis of both deities, but in the case of Parjanya this is more evident.

The derivation of this word is not clear. In the Ṛgveda, by this form both the conventional meaning, the cloud, and the deity presiding over the cloud are intended. In the Brāhmaṇas too both these meanings appear to be current. In the Mahābhārata Indra and Parjanya are both regarded as one.

The main purport of this sūkta has been described by the Western scholar Muir in the following way:

##### English translation
1. "Address the powerful (god) with these words; laud Parjanya; worship him
with reverenc: the procreative and stimulating fructifier, resounding, sheds his seed and
impregnates the plants.
2. He splits the trees, he destroys the Rakshasas ; the whole creation is afraid of
the mighty stroke ; even the innocent man flees before the vigorous god, when Parjanya
thundering smites the evil doers.

### Printed p. 203

1. Like a charioteer urging forward his horses with a whip, the god brings into view his showery scouts. From afar the lion's roarings arise, when Parjanya charges the clouds with rain.
4. The winds blow. the lightnings fall, the plants shoot up ; the heaven fructifies; food is produced for all created things, when Parjanya, thundering, replenishes the earth with moisture.
5. Parjanya, before whose agency the earth bows down, at whose operation all hoofed cattle quiver ; by whose action plants (spring up) of every form; grant your mighty protection.
6. Gran to us, Maruts, the rain of the sky ; replenish the streams of the procreative horse ; come hither with this your thunder, our divine father, shedding waters.
7. Resound, thunder, Impregnate, rush hither and thither with your watery chariot. Draw on forward with you. your opened and inverted water-skin : let the hills and dale be levelled
8. Raise aloft your vast water-vessel, and pour down showers ; let the discharged rivulets roll on forward : mosisten the heaven and earth with fatness ; lat there be well-filled drinking places for the cows.
9. When you, Parjanya, resounding and thundering, slay the evil-doers, the whole universe rejoices, whatever lives upon the earth.
10. You have shed down rain : now desist ; you have made the waterless wastes fit to be traversed ; you have generated plants for food, and you have fulfilled the desires of living creatures.

**NOTE**

(The introduction ends.)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अच्छा वदेति दशर्चमेकादशं सूक्तं भौमस्यात्रेरार्षं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका । अच्छ दशात्रिः पार्जन्यमुपाद्यास्तिस्रो जगत्य उपांत्यानुष्टुबिति । यत्पर्जन्येत्येषा नवम्यनुष्टुप् वि वृक्षानित्याद्यास्तिस्रो जगत्यः शिष्टाः षट् त्रिष्टुभः ।
> पर्जन्यो देवता ॥ अनेन सूक्तेन प्रत्यृचं वा दिश उपस्थेयाः । सूत्रितं च ।
> संस्थितायां सर्वा दिश उपतिष्ठेताच्छा वद तवसं गीर्भिराभिरिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं
> सूक्तेन वा । आ- २-१३ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning अच्छा वद (*acchā vada*) has ten Ṛks and is the eleventh sūkta; its seer is Atri, son of Bhūmi. On it the Anukramaṇikā says: अच्छ दशात्रिः पार्जन्यमुपाद्यास्तिस्रो जगत्य उपांत्यानुष्टुबिति । (*accha daśātriḥ pārjanyamupādyāstisro jagatya upāṃtyānuṣṭubiti |*). The deity is Parjanya, and three Ṛks, beginning with the first, are Jagatī, the penultimate being Anuṣṭubh. The ninth Ṛk, यत्पर्जन्य (*yatparjanya*), is Anuṣṭubh; the three beginning वि वृक्षानि (*vi vṛkṣāni*) are Jagatī; the remaining six are Triṣṭubh. The deity is Parjanya. Ritual use: with this sūkta, or with each Ṛk, the directions are to be addressed. The sūtra says: संस्थितायां सर्वा दिश उपतिष्ठेताच्छा वद तवसं गीर्भिराभिरिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं सूक्तेन वा । (*saṃsthitāyāṃ sarvā diśa upatiṣṭhetācchā vada tavasaṃ gīrbhirābhiriti catasṛbhiḥ pratyṛcaṃ sūktena vā |*) (Ā. 2-13). **NOTE**
